# MAKEPOSSEQ_PLAN — MakePosSequence by physics

The command `MakePosSequence` turns a multi-phase circuit into its positive-sequence equivalent. The rules of this plan are its own: the user decided them on 2026-10-04 from two research runs and refined them through 2026-10-16, section 1 states them in full with the book behind each item (the stated limits in section 6), and `RULINGS_QUEUE.md` records the dates and the queue. This plan builds them in ten steps, `MPS-01` to `MPS-10`, each sized for one lane and one run of the local step script (section 3). It replaces three rows of the queue, which section 5 names. It is written for branch `update` at `73a364f9`, which holds step 3 of the queue (the matrix reduction, squash commit `b6517a73`), queue step 2 (a rating that is not set is none, `66839773`), queue step 4 (LineGeometry rules, `4212d57f`) and the queue register. Each step's `base` is the sha of `update` at its launch.

How to read it. Section 1 is the binding procedure with every formula and the book behind it. Section 2 holds the decisions. Section 3 holds the ritual and the launch checklist. Section 4 holds the shared conventions and the steps, each in the same form. Sections 5 to 9 hold the order, the stated limits, the coverage, the four questions put to the user with their decisions and what is out of scope. An executor of a step reads sections 0 to 3, the conventions at the head of section 4, its own step section, section 5, the rows of sections 6 and 7 that name its step, and sections 8 and 9, and nothing else of this plan: the step brief the coordinator builds holds exactly these (section 3).

## 0. Binding rules

- `CLAUDE.md` is authoritative. Ritual step 0 first: `.inputs/dss_capi` (186 `.pas` files), `.inputs/electricdss-tst` and `.inputs/electricdss-code-r4133-trunk` present and non-empty, else stop and report. `(Get-Command cargo).Source` lies under `.cargo\bin` before any gate is trusted.
- Physics is the only authority. A rule here comes from a derivation and a measured balance of complex power, never from what r4133 or dss_capi does. No upstream bug is reproduced in any lane. Every divergence from an oracle channel is excluded field by field (`tests/corpus/ledger.json`, `crates/dss-test-harness/src/harness/lane.rs`, golden fix-ups) and pinned by its own expected-value test.
- The DSS language specification is the OpenDSS documentation (`Version8/Distrib/Doc/OpenDSS Documentation.chm` of the r4133 trunk). The plan adds no syntax, no intent heuristic and no warning level. A refusal of input that breaks the specification is no divergence.
- Sections 1 and 2 state every rule and decision of this plan in full, and nothing is incorporated by reference. Section 1 is the binding procedure and may not be weakened: every item names its book or the words "own derivation", and section 1.15 sorts the procedure into what the books confirm and what is own derivation. Some values differ from what a plain reading of the procedure suggests, and the plan gives each with its computation at its place: section 6 on the inter-circuit term (stated limit L1: the exactness condition on the Kron-reduced inter-circuit block, the mirrored phase order, and the lost reactive power with its active part), section 1.13 on the units of an open-delta bank and on a bank of one-phase units between one pair of ports, section 1.14 on the excitation of the two-phase Line, and section 1.13 with decision D17 and stated limit L11 on the written power of an element whose legs sit at different per-unit voltages, which is not the rated power over 3 (own derivation, with the joint-star factor `f` of section 1.6 item 4). Where the rest lives: the order check applies only to the elements whose model depends on the order (section 1.4 item 3, section 8 Q4), the phase selector and the power-factor guards of a control on a reduced circuit are decision D22 (MPS-10), a source of more than three phases and a lone group that is no balanced set stop the command in MPS-02 (section 1.4 item 1, section 1.12 cases 2 and 3), decision D19 answers the nine questions of the step-3 row of `docs/upgrade/DIVERGENCES.md` and the reading of a phase-to-phase reactor after the reduction, MPS-01 item 9 writes the "reads the loss of" sentence of that row, and the Capacitor's delta and `cmatrix` filter stamp belongs to queue step 20 (section 1.13).
- The user's standing decisions carry over: the rating of merged cores is confirmed (section 1.10, decision D6), the four-conductor line with its neutral retained (open question 8 of step 3) belongs to this plan, a lane lands as one squash commit whose subject names the code change, every settlement is audited by a fresh agent, no trailer lines in commits, texts state the present, no `file.rs:N` citation into the repository, no scratch file as provenance, no OpenDSS or Pascal citation in code, no semicolon joining two sentences.
- No test is deleted or ignored. A step-3 pin whose rule this plan replaces is rewritten in place under a name that states the new rule, on the same deck where the deck still applies, and the step lists the old and the new name.
- Every OpenDSS bug a step fixes gets a report in `investigations/to_opendss/`, written by that step and verified before the landing. The coordinator reserves the numbers 100 to 110 for this plan when it adopts it (the next eleven free numbers if some are taken by then, in the same order), and each step uses only the numbers its section names, so two lanes of one wave never take the same number. A step that extends report 82 adds its sections there. A fault r4133 does not have (measured with epri-worker) gets no report: the step adds it to `investigations/to_opendss/NOT-APPLICABLE-TO-R4133.md` with the measurement, as the earlier dss_capi-only faults are recorded.
- Models: every agent runs on opus with an explicit effort. Fable is not used.
- Tolerances are never loosened. The classes the pins use:
  - T1, identity: a closed form against the general code path, or a written value against the formula evaluated on the deck's own data, `1e-12` relative.
  - T2, book value: against a printed number, half a unit of its last printed digit, unless the pin states a larger book rounding with its cause.
  - T3, balance of an exact case: `dS/S` at or below `balance::BALANCE_REL = 1e-9` (the step-3 helper in `elements::pos_seq::balance`).
  - T4, reference value: an engine solve against the numpy reference of section 1.16, `1e-6` relative (one linear circuit solved by two codes).
  - T5, size of a stated limit or of a wrong reading: the engine measures it, the pin holds it to two significant digits (within half a unit of the second digit of the value the engine gives at the step's run) and holds the wrong reading above a stated floor. The plan gives every T5 number with its recipe. The numpy figure of the plan must agree with the engine's to those two digits, else the executor stops and reports.
  - T6, bound: the engine's value lies below a stated bound and the step records the engine's value. Used where a size sits near the solver's floor (the nine-core merge, 2.8e-9, the cable loss of K2), where the plan gives the value its recipe measured, and for the whole feeder of MPS-09, whose parts are each measured in their own pins: there the plan gives the sizes of the parts as the argument of the bound, and no recipe measures the whole deck. Also used for a balance that a stamp defect outside this plan sets, until the queue step that owns the defect lands (the Capacitor filter stamp of section 1.13, queue step 20).
- Provenance in committed text is a book with section, equation and printed page, or the sentence "own derivation, recipe R-n" with the recipe restated where it is short. The research behind section 1 is local and is never cited. The local step tooling (`.inputs/workflows`) is named only as tooling, never as provenance.

## 1. Authority and the procedure

### 1.0 Notation

| symbol | meaning |
|---|---|
| `a` | `exp(j 2π/3)` |
| `v` | `(1, a², a)ᵀ`, the unit phasors of a three-phase port in positive order |
| node | one node `k` of one bus, written `bus.k`. Node 0 of every bus is earth |
| value `x` of a node | the balanced no-load voltage the map carries to that node, per unit of the phase voltage of the seed that reached it (section 1.2): sources give their pattern, Lines and `Through` legs copy it, windings scale it by their ratio. 0 on earth |
| phasor `w` of a node | `x/|x|`, the unit complex number of the node's balanced voltage. Earth, internal nodes and a node with `|x|` at or below `1e-9` of the largest `|x|` of its solve carry none (`w = 0` in `W`) |
| phase node | a node that carries a phasor once the map is complete |
| port | a set of phase nodes of one bus that the reduced circuit represents by one node. A port holds each phasor at most once, and its phasors are pairwise 120 degrees apart (one, two or three of them) or are two poles 180 degrees apart |
| internal node | a node that is neither earth nor a port node. It obeys the current law and is eliminated |
| private node | a node that the conductors of one element alone touch, disabled elements counted |
| circuit `c` of an element | the conductors of one element that join one port at terminal 1 to one port at terminal 2, or one port to earth |
| `N_c` | number of distinct phasors (phase nodes) of circuit `c`: 1, 2 or 3 |
| `M` | number of circuits of the reduced element, written as its `phases=` |
| `W` | phasor matrix of an element: one row per kept conductor (or per node of a nodal matrix), one column per circuit (or per port), entry the phasor of that conductor's nodes, 0 on earth and internal nodes |
| `G` | `Wᴴ W = diag(N_c)` |
| `U` | one complex voltage per reduced node. The balanced state is `V = W U` |
| `Z`, `C`, `Y` | series impedance matrix (total ohm), nodal capacitance matrix (total farad), nodal admittance matrix (siemens) of a set of conductors |
| `Zb`, `Cb` | `Z` and `C` after parallel conductors are merged |
| `A` | merge incidence: one row per conductor, one column per merged conductor, 1 where the conductor belongs to the group, else 0 |
| `S(X)` | `(1/3) vᴴ X v`, the positive-sequence self term of a three-phase matrix. For a symmetric `X` it is the mean diagonal minus the mean off-diagonal entry. A non-symmetric `X` (a source or Reactor stamp with `Z2 ≠ Z1`) gives its positive-sequence eigenvalue, not the value of its symmetric part |
| `Z1`, `Y1`, `C1` | the reduced matrices, `M x M` |
| `S12`, `A12` | `(Z1[1,2] + Z1[2,1])/2` and `(Z1[1,2] - Z1[2,1])/2` |
| leg | one branch of an element from one of its conductors (`plus`) to another (`minus`) or to earth (`minus` none, for an element that injects or draws per conductor to earth with no neutral conductor, as the VCCS): a winding of one phase, a leg of a Load or other power conversion element, a branch of a Reactor, Capacitor, Fault or GICTransformer. `V_leg` is its rated voltage as the stamp uses it, `S_leg` the complex power it draws at `V_leg` in the element's nominal admittance (generation negative) |
| `λ` | the phasor across one leg from the map, `w(plus end) - w(minus end)`, 0 on earth and on internal nodes |
| `ν` | the voltage of an eliminated star node in the balanced state per unit port voltage (sections 1.5 and 1.6) |
| `Δw` | the pattern voltage across one leg per unit port voltage: `λ` with each star node at its `ν` in place of 0 |
| `dS/S` | `|S_reduced - S_full| / |S_full|` on the complex power of `Vsource.source` at terminal 1, the reduced reading being the engine's positive-sequence report (three times one phase) |

Every formula of this plan uses Fortescue's amplitude-invariant transformation, `A⁻¹ = (1/3) Aᴴ` (Tleis eq. (2.13), p. 33): a reduced voltage is the phase voltage and a reduced current the phase current of a three-phase port, and the three-phase power is three times the one-phase product (Tleis eq. (2.18), p. 34, Fortescue (1918) eq. (112), p. 1064). The normalised transformation of Dommel (p. 4-47, footnote) and the power-invariant one give the same impedances with sequence voltages and currents `√3` times those here, so a voltage or current expression taken from such a book is divided by `√3` before it enters a pin.

The reduced circuit is one phase of three. The engine reports every reduced power three times, so a reduced element of power `S'` stands for `3 S'`. Every power a control or a register reads through `elements::traits::CktElement::terminal_power` is that balanced total, since the method multiplies the reduced terminal's power by 3 when `positive_sequence` is set. An element's own rated and present powers (its `kw` and `kvar`, the `kw_base` of a Generator, the `present_kw` of a Storage) stay per reduced phase, a third of the balanced total they stand for. Decision D22 converts between the two where a control moves an element's power or compares a reading with the element's own power.

### 1.1 Principle

The reduced circuit keeps the complex power of every balanced state `V = W U` of the multi-phase circuit, `W` holding the true phasor of every port node. No class has a rule of its own: every element is reduced by the same projection of what it stamps.

Derivation (own derivation on the books' balanced-state definition):
- Voltage imposed on a nodal matrix `Y`: the element draws `S = Vᵀ conj(Y V)`, so `conj(S) = Vᴴ Y V = Uᴴ Wᴴ Y W U`. The reduced element of matrix `Y1` stands for `3 Uᵀ conj(Y1 U)`, whose conjugate is `3 Uᴴ Y1 U`. Equal for every `U` gives `Y1 = (1/3) Wᴴ Y W`.
- Current imposed on a series set `Z`: a circuit of `N_c` phases carries `I = W i`, its reduced conductor carries `i' = (N_c/3) i` (the reduced power `3 U conj(i')` equals the circuit power `N_c U conj(i)`), and `iᴴ Wᴴ Z W i = 3 i'ᴴ Z1 i'` gives `Z1 = 3 G⁻¹ (Wᴴ Z W) G⁻¹`.

Books: the balanced state as the condition that defines the positive-sequence impedance, Dommel, EMTP Theory Book, eq. (4.34), p. 4-24, and Tleis, Power Systems Modelling and Fault Analysis (2008), eq. (2.26a), p. 38. The invariance of complex power under a current transformation `I = K I'`, which forces `V' = Kᴴ V` and `Z' = Kᴴ Z K`, is Anderson, Analysis of Faulted Power Systems, section 8.5, p. 285, and Grainger and Stevenson, Power System Analysis (1994), section 8.6, pp. 311 to 313, eq. (8.55) to (8.63): the impedance form is that theorem with `K = 3 W G⁻¹` and the reporting factor 1/3. The reporting factor, the admittance-form dual with a general `W` and "no class has a rule of its own" are own derivation.

### 1.2 Node meaning and phasors

Node 0 is earth. Line conductors and transformer windings carry phasors from the source, conductor by conductor. A winding adds the displacement and polarity of its connection. A source's own angle never enters the map. Own derivation. The books leave the transformer shift out of the positive-sequence network and name the exception of a closed loop (Grainger and Stevenson, Power System Analysis, section 2.6, p. 68, and section 11.10, p. 462, Tleis section 4.2.3, pp. 214 and 223). The neutral as the reference of the positive-sequence network, which the zero-mean row of a floating set below states, is Grainger section 11.10, p. 462 ("Neutral is the reference for voltages in positive- and negative-sequence circuits"), and Clarke Vol. I p. 312 ("All neutral points are therefore points of zero potential in the α and β networks").

Data. For every circuit element, enabled or disabled (decision D9), and every terminal, the node of each conductor by the rule `Circuit::process_bus_defs` applies: conductor `i < nphases` defaults to node `i + 1`, the others to 0, and an explicit `.n` list overrides. MPS-02 moves that rule into one pure function that both callers use, so the map reads exactly the nodes the solve builds. A node is the pair (lowercase bus name, node number). The legs of every element come from the trait method `elements::traits::CktElement::pos_seq_legs` (default `None`), which returns `PosSeqLeg { kind, winding, phase, plus, minus, v_leg, s_leg }` with `plus: usize` and `minus: Option<usize>` conductor indices over all terminals in the element's conductor order (`minus` is `None` for a leg to earth), so the map reads each leg's nodes from the terminal node lists and needs no knowledge of a class's internal node aliasing. `kind` is one of:
- `Winding`: one phase of one transformer winding, an ideal-transformer leg of rated voltage `v_leg` (`Winding::vbase`, with its sign).
- `Through`: two conductors that carry one value, as a Line conductor does: the series winding of an AutoTrans (from its H node to its X node of one phase: an autotransformer puts no displacement between its terminals, `kVH/kVX` is real and positive, its high- and low-voltage systems being electrically connected in star, IEC 60076-8:1997, 3.1, p. 19, and 3.4, p. 21, Grainger section 2.7, pp. 71, 72), and the conductor pairs of a series element (GICLine, UPFC and GICsource from terminal 1 to terminal 2, a Vsource or Isource whose terminal 2 lies on nodes other than earth). A source whose terminal 2 is earth has no `Through` leg.
- `Lumped`: a branch of a Reactor, Capacitor, Fault or GICTransformer, or a leg of a power conversion element (Load, Generator, PVSystem, Storage, WindGen, IndMach012, VCCS, the AC conductors of a VSConverter).

MPS-01 implements `pos_seq_legs` for every class but the two transformer classes, MPS-02 for Transformer and AutoTrans (section 4, MPS-01 item 6 and MPS-02 item 3).

The kind of every class, which the map reads (MPS-02 item 2):

| kind | classes |
|---|---|
| Line | Line |
| lumped PD | Reactor, Capacitor, Fault, GICTransformer |
| transformer | Transformer, AutoTrans |
| source | Vsource, Isource |
| power conversion | Load, Generator, PVSystem, Storage, WindGen, IndMach012, VCCS, VSConverter |
| series element | UPFC, GICLine, GICsource |
| control or meter | every class under `elements/control` and `elements/meter`, and the Fuse (`elements/pd/fuse`, a control with no Y stamp) |

A class with no row has no legs and no terminals the map reads. A class added later without a row is refused with the D10 message until it gets one.

Algorithm (MPS-02, module `exec/make_pos_seq/map.rs`). The map carries the value `x` of every node, and normalises it to the phasor `w = x/|x|` where it compares phasors and where `W` is built:
1. Seeds. Walk the Vsources in creation order. A Vsource whose terminal-1 phase nodes carry no value yet seeds them with the pattern of its phase count: three phases `(1, a², a)` in conductor order, two phases `(1, -1)` (its poles stand 180 degrees apart, `pc/vsource/source.rs`), one phase `(1)`. A Vsource of more than three phases is refused (case 2), as is an Isource of more than three phases when its terminal is grouped (section 1.4 item 1). A Vsource whose nodes all carry values already does not seed and is projected (section 1.9). A Vsource some of whose terminal-1 nodes carry values and others none seeds the others with its pattern turned to the phasor of its first carried node and scaled to its value (its internal phase relation, never its own angle), then is projected. After each seed, run step 2 to exhaustion.
2. Strong carriers, repeated until no node changes. Earth never gives and never takes a value.
   - Line conductor `k` and every `Through` leg: its two end nodes carry the same value. A value flows from a node that has one to a node that has none and is not earth. A roll of the nodes between two buses is carried.
   - Transformers (Transformer and AutoTrans, their `Winding` legs), solved jointly. A group is the set of transformers whose legs touch a node without a value, joined while two of them share such a node. A group is solved when one of its legs touches a node that carries a value or earth. In each transformer the reference winding `r` is the first winding in winding order with a leg end that carries a value or is earth, else its first winding. For every other winding `n` and phase `p` the ideal transformer imposes the row `x(plus_n,p) - x(minus_n,p) - ρ_n (x(plus_r,p) - x(minus_r,p)) = 0` with `ρ_n = V_leg,n / V_leg,r`. Known nodes are fixed at their value, earth at 0, every other node of the group's legs is an unknown. Two more kinds of row close the system:
     - one zero-mean row `Σ x = 0` per floating set: a set of unknown nodes connected to each other through the group's legs that touches no known node and no earth (a floating delta secondary built of one-phase units, an open-delta pair, a floating wye winding with its star). It is the solution the balanced state takes, with no zero-sequence voltage on a floating set (the neutral as the reference, Grainger p. 462, Clarke Vol. I p. 312),
     - one star row per wye winding of two or more legs whose common `minus` node is unknown: `x(star) = (1/n) Σ_k x(plus_k)` over its `n` legs (the balanced star of equal legs, an ungrounded-wye winding on known phase nodes).
     The rows are solved by least squares through the normal equations `Aᴴ A x = Aᴴ b` with `elements::pos_seq::project::lu_solve` (MPS-01, partial pivoting, the same in both lanes). A dependent row (a star row on a floating winding repeats its winding rows) changes nothing. A group whose normal matrix is singular (`lu_solve` returns `None`) has an unknown that no row fixes, as the primary `x.1.2` of a one-phase unit that only a series reactor feeds, beside a secondary to earth, or the far end `b.5` of a primary on `b.1.5` that nothing else reaches. Such a group waits: none of its nodes takes a value, and it is solved again whenever a later stage gives one of its nodes a value (step 3 runs step 2 again). When the map is complete (after step 6), a group still singular whose rows hold a known value (a leg touches a node that carries a value) leaves a node with no balanced voltage, and the command stops (section 1.12, the undetermined winding), naming the first unknown node of the group in walk order and its first transformer in creation order. A group still singular whose rows hold no known value touches earth and unknown nodes only, an island no source reaches, and its nodes stay without a value (decision D13). A node whose `|x|` lies at or below `1e-9` of the largest `|x|` of the group carries no value, so the star of a three-leg winding comes out at 0. Instances: wye-wye 0 degrees, a reversed winding 180 degrees (the clock number of a one-phase transformer is 0 or 6, IEC 60076-1:2011, 7.2.2, p. 38), delta-wye ±30 degrees by the transformer's own stamp (its `LeadLag`: the default lagging bank puts the wye secondary at -30 degrees), a one-phase winding between two phases `(1 - a²)/√3` in phasor, a centre-tapped secondary `(ℓ, -ℓ)`, three one-phase units from `b.p` to earth into a delta `x.1.2`, `x.2.3`, `x.3.1` at 330, 210, 90 degrees, an open-delta pair `b.1.2 → r.1.2` and `b.3.2 → r.3.2` at 0, 240, 120 degrees.
3. Lumped stage (section 1.3). Whenever it gives a node a value, run step 2 again.
4. Stop when a full round changes nothing.
5. Consistency, checked while carrying and after step 4, on phasors: two phasors are equal when `|w1 - w2| <= 1e-9`. Values of one phasor and different magnitude (two paths through windings of different ratio) are no conflict.
   - A Line conductor whose two ends carry different phasors: case 4 when both ends lie on one bus (a conductor between two phases), else case 1 (the node at its terminal-2 end receives a second phasor, named with the carrier that gave it the first). A `Through` leg whose ends carry different phasors: case 1. A `Through` leg with an end on earth is not checked.
   - A Line conductor with one end on earth and the other on a node that carries a phasor: case 4.
   - A `Winding` leg whose two ends both carry values must satisfy `x(plus) - x(minus) = κ ℓ_p` with `ℓ_p = x(plus_r,p) - x(minus_r,p)` the reference leg of its phase and `κ` real and positive within `1e-9` relative, else case 1 naming the leg's plus node and the transformer.
6. A multi-phase terminal of a power conversion element whose nodes carry no value after step 4 seeds `(1, a², a)` cut to its phase count (two or three phases: a terminal above three phases is no balanced set and stops the command, case 3, section 1.4 item 1), then steps 2 to 4 run again (decision D13). Nodes left without a value are internal.

### 1.3 Lumped conductors

A node without a phasor that two-terminal conductors of Reactors, Capacitors, Faults, Loads and Generators reach with one phasor becomes a port node of that phasor. A node they reach with different phasors, and every node left over, is an internal node. Own derivation.

Algorithm (MPS-02). A lumped conductor is a `Lumped` leg (decision D11 counts every power conversion class: a leg of a PVSystem is a two-terminal conductor as a leg of a Load is). After the strong carriers are exhausted the lumped stage runs in rounds:
1. A node without a phasor that two lumped conductors reach from nodes of two different phasors becomes internal at once.
2. Any other node without a phasor is decided in a round when every lumped conductor at it that counts leads to earth or to a node that held a phasor at the start of the round: one distinct phasor among them makes it a port node of that phasor (it takes the value of the node that gave it), and none (every counted conductor leads to earth) makes it internal. A node with a counted lumped conductor to a node that is still undecided waits. A leg between two conductors of one multi-phase terminal of one element (a delta leg, a leg to the element's own star on that terminal) does not count for the nodes of that terminal: such a node is decided by its other lumped conductors, and the terminal's nodes form one port by section 1.4. Legs between two terminals of an element and legs of separate elements always count.
3. Every node decided in a round takes its meaning at once, then the strong carriers run again (section 1.2 step 2).
4. When a round decides nothing, every waiting node is internal.

A lumped conductor between two nodes that both carry a phasor is a branch and changes no meaning. The outcome does not depend on the order of the elements: a chain `b.1 - x.1 - y.1 - b.2` of three reactors leaves `x.1` and `y.1` waiting on each other, so both are internal and the chain is one joint star (section 1.6), while `b.1 - b.4` with `b.4 - b.0` makes `b.4` a port node of phasor 0. A three-phase series Reactor from `b` to `c` with a delta Load on `c` and nothing else there makes `c` one port of `b`'s phasors: the delta legs of the Load do not count, so each node of `c` is decided by the reactor branch that reaches it.

### 1.4 Ports

Phase nodes bound by one multi-phase terminal are one port. Other phase nodes follow the conductor order of the Line that reaches them, three by three. A port holds each phasor once.

Algorithm (MPS-02, module `exec/make_pos_seq/ports.rs`):
1. Groups. A Vsource or Isource of more than three phases is refused (case 2). A multi-phase terminal of a Vsource, Isource, transformer winding or power conversion element groups its phase nodes (decision D11), and the group is checked as it forms: a group that is not one balanced set (notation: each phasor once, pairwise 120 degrees apart or two poles), as the four-node terminal of a power conversion element that nothing else reaches, stops the command (case 3) naming its element alone. A passive PD element (Line, Reactor, Capacitor, Fault, GICTransformer) groups the phase nodes it reaches at each terminal by its conductor order, three by three: walking its conductors at that terminal, a new group starts at the first conductor, and the next node joins the current group only while the group stays one balanced set, that is, while the group holds fewer than three nodes and the next node's phasor stands 120 degrees from every phasor in it, or the group is one node and the next stands 180 degrees from it (a two-pole pair, which then closes). Otherwise the next node starts a new group. A node already in a group of this element (parallel cores, several conductors on one node) adds nothing. A port node that no group holds is a group of its own. Never by node number. On a six-conductor Line whose second circuit stands behind a reversed bank (`b.1.2.3` at 0, 240, 120 and `b.4.5.6` at 180, 60, 300 degrees) or behind a delta-wye bank (330, 210, 90 degrees) the walk closes the first group at three nodes and gives two ports.
2. Ports. Groups of one bus that share a node are joined, until no two of them share a node. A three-phase Line into `c` and a one-phase Line out of `c.1` give one port `{c.1, c.2, c.3}`, and so do two two-phase Lines on `c.1.2` and `c.2.3`. The joins run over the groups in the creation order of their elements, terminal by terminal, each group joining every port formed so far that it shares a node with. A port stays one balanced set (notation): the first join that gives a port one phasor twice, or phasors that are neither pairwise 120 degrees apart nor two poles, stops the command (case 3), naming the element of the earliest group in that port and the element of the group being joined. Every subset of a balanced set is balanced, so checking each join is the same as checking the final port. A two-pole group `{b.1, b.4}` (0 and 180 degrees, `b.4` behind a reversed winding) joined to the three-phase port `{b.1, b.2, b.3}` is refused, since the conductors on `b.1` and `b.4` would land on one reduced node.
3. Order. An element whose model depends on the order of its conductors must see on its port, in its own conductor order, the pattern its model imposes, up to one common unit factor:
   - Vsource: `(1, a², a)` for three phases, `(1, -1)` for two (its poles), `(1)` for one,
   - Isource: `(1, a², a)` for three phases, `(1, a²)` for two (its injections stand at 0 and -120 degrees in conductor order),
   - VSConverter: `exp(-j 2π k / n_ac)` over its `n_ac` AC conductors, `(1, a², a)` for three and `(1, -1)` for two (its AC sources turn by `360/n_ac` degrees from conductor to conductor),
   - IndMach012: `(1, a², a)` (it computes in sequence quantities),
   - a three-phase PVSystem or Storage with `Balanced=yes`, and a three-phase Generator of `model=7` with `Balanced=yes`: `(1, a², a)` (each keeps only the positive-sequence voltage of its own conductor order).
   Otherwise the command stops (case 2). Every other element (a Load, a Generator, PVSystem or Storage without that flag, a WindGen, a VCCS, a transformer winding, every passive element) draws the same balanced power in either order, `Y1 = (1/3) Σ_k |Δw_k|² y` over its equal legs, and is reduced on whatever order its port shows: a three-phase Load on `c.1.3.2` is reduced exactly to `c`, and a winding on `c.1.3.2` carries the reversed order to its far side, where the order check of the elements there applies.
4. Numbering. Ports of a bus are numbered 1, 2, ... in the order of their lowest original node number (decision D12). Port `j` of a bus becomes node `j` of that bus in the reduced circuit (section 1.11).

Books: the grouping of conductors three by three as a convention, Dommel section 4.1.4, p. 4-48, Kersting, Distribution System Modeling and Analysis, third edition (CRC Press 2012), eq. (4.83), p. 96 (partitioned "between the third and fourth rows and columns"), Anderson section 4.15, eq. (4.265), p. 139 (`A_2 = diag(A, A)` on the six-conductor matrix). A static element presents the same impedance to positive- and negative-sequence currents, so only a model that depends on the order is checked for it (Tleis section 4.2.3, p. 214: "the impedance such plant presents to the flow of PPS or NPS currents is independent of the phase rotation", Grainger section 11.10, p. 461). Binding by terminals first, the check of a group as it forms, the join of groups with its balanced-set check and the order check by the element's own pattern are own derivation.

### 1.5 Internal nodes inside one element

Internal nodes obey the current law and are eliminated with the passive elements on them. Partition the conductors of a Line into kept `u` (phase conductors) and eliminated `g` (every other conductor), classified by the meaning of their two end nodes:

| conductor ends | in `Z` | in `C` | book |
|---|---|---|---|
| earth at both ends | Kron: `Z_red = Z_uu - Z_ug Z_gg⁻¹ Z_gu` | struck: `C_red = C_uu` | Dommel section 4.1.2.1, eq. (4.28a), (4.28b), pp. 4-18, 4-19, Kersting eq. (4.55), p. 86, and eq. (5.13), p. 123 (the Kron reduction of the potential coefficients, which is the strike of `C`), Tleis eq. (3.32b), p. 92, Hase eq. (23.5), p. 554 (the sheath earthed at both ends, `L1 = (Ls - Lm) - δ1`), Clarke Vol. I p. 421 (a multigrounded neutral "can be treated as a ground wire") |
| private nodes at both ends | struck: `Z_red = Z_uu` | Kron: `C_red = C_uu - C_ug C_gg⁻¹ C_gu` | `Z`: Tleis p. 160 (`IS = 0`), Clarke Vol. II p. 74. `C`: own derivation |
| earth at one end, private node at the other | struck | struck | Dommel section 4.1.2.5 (b), p. 4-30 ("ignores them in the calculation of the series impedance matrix ... but takes them into account in the calculation of the capacitance matrix"), Hase eq. (23.4), p. 553 (the sheath earthed at one end, no sheath current, `L1 = Ls - Lm`) |
| an end on an internal node that another element touches | a chain, section 1.6 | section 1.6 | section 1.6 |

A star node of one element (the terminal-2 nodes of a wye bank on a private node, as `bus2=b.4.4.4`, or the neutral of a wye Load on `b.1.2.3.4` with `b.4` touched by nothing else) is Kron-reduced in the element's nodal matrix, `Y_red = Y_kk - Y_ks Y_ss⁻¹ Y_sk`, a power conversion element by its nominal admittance (decision D14) (Clarke Vol. I p. 275, eq. [66], [67]). Its voltage per unit port voltage is `ν = -Y_ss⁻¹ Y_sk W`. A star node made of several elements is eliminated jointly (section 1.6). Elimination precedes the merge and the merge precedes the projection (Kersting pp. 88 and 91, Anderson section 4.10.1, Dommel section 4.1.2).

### 1.6 Chains and joint stars: internal nodes shared by several elements

Internal nodes that join the non-phase conductors of several Lines (cable screens, cross-bonding, a neutral or earth wire through internal nodes) are eliminated jointly, each Line receiving the share of its own section (decision D1, K2). A star node made of several lumped elements is eliminated jointly by its nodal matrix.

A chain is a connected set of internal nodes, other than nodes private to one element, together with every non-phase Line conductor and every lumped branch that joins two of them or one of them to earth. Earth is a fixed node at 0 V.

Chain, series part (MPS-05, module `exec/make_pos_seq/chains.rs`):
1. Excitation columns. Every circuit of every Line with a conductor in the chain is a column. Two Line circuits of the chain that follow each other through a port where no other Line circuit of the chain meets them are one column, one current through the sections, also when a load or another element is tapped at that port (stated limit L8). At a port where three or more Line circuits of the chain meet, each keeps its own column and the terms one column induces in the Lines of another column are dropped (stated limit L8 as well).
2. Unknowns per column: the current of every non-phase Line conductor in the chain and the voltage of every chain node.
3. Equations per column at unit column current: the phase conductors of each Line circuit in the column carry `I_p = w_c` (the circuit's phasors), all other phase conductors carry 0. Each non-phase conductor `e` of Line `ℓ` from node `x` to node `y`: `Σ_f Z_ef I_f + Σ_p Z_ep I_p = V_x - V_y` (sums over the Line's non-phase and phase conductors, total ohm, 0 V on earth). Each lumped branch of admittance `y_b` from `x` to `y` carries `y_b (V_x - V_y)`, a power conversion element between two chain nodes by its nominal admittance. Current law at every chain node.
4. A chain that reaches earth nowhere has its node voltages fixed only up to a common constant, which enters no drop and no reduced value, and its current-law rows sum to zero. The row `V = 0` on its first chain node (lowest bus name, then lowest node number) replaces the current-law row of that same node, so the system stays square and full rank.
5. Solve. The system is assembled densely per chain (tens of unknowns) and solved by `elements::pos_seq::project::lu_solve` (partial pivoting, one right-hand side per excitation column). `X_ℓ` holds the solved non-phase currents of Line `ℓ`, one column per excitation column.
6. Each Line's phase drop per unit column current: `D_ℓ = Z_pp,ℓ I_p + Z_pe,ℓ X_ℓ`. Its reduced matrix entry between its circuits `c` and `d`: `Z1_ℓ[c,d] = (3 / (N_c N_d)) w_cᴴ D_ℓ[:, col(d)]`, the symmetric part written (section 1.10).
7. Kron (`X = -Z_ee⁻¹ Z_ep W`, a conductor on earth at both ends of one Line) and strike (`X = 0`, a conductor with a private end) are the degenerate cases of this one solve, which is why section 1.5 is its instance for one element.
8. Lumped branches and power conversion elements with both ends on chain nodes, or on a chain node and earth, touch no port: they are disabled (section 1.11) and their losses sit in the Lines' shares through `Z_pe X`. A lumped or power conversion conductor between a port node and a chain node reads that chain node at zero volts and enters the solve as an open conductor (decision D14, stated limit L9). Its reduced value is then the one it has on earth.
9. A section's share is a share of the chain's passive total, not a passive element by itself. With strongly unequal minor sections the sheath current the whole chain carries feeds power back into the cores of a short section, whose share then has a negative real part: the Tleis 3.5 cable in sections of 0.9, 0.05 and 0.05 km gives `0.03905 + j0.13021`, `-0.00151 + j0.12304` and `0.01256 + j0.16175` ohm/km. The Line is written as computed, the criterion of section 1.12 holds for the chain as a whole (`Σ_k Z1_k len_k` is passive and keeps the source power), and MPS-05 pins it.

Chain, shunt part: a chain that reaches earth (a conductor or a lumped branch of it ends on node 0) has its conductors struck in `C` (zero potential, the electrostatic reading of Dommel p. 4-30). A chain that reaches earth nowhere is one equipotential body with zero total charge: per port column, its potential is `φ = -(Σ_ℓ 1ᵀ C_ep,ℓ w) / (Σ_ℓ 1ᵀ C_ee,ℓ 1)` and each Line's `C1_ℓ = (1/3) Wᴴ (C_pp,ℓ W + C_pe,ℓ 1 φ)`. For a single Line on private nodes this is the Kron reduction of section 1.5.

Joint star of lumped elements (internal nodes that only lumped and power conversion conductors reach, no Line conductor, MPS-05):
1. Assemble the nodal admittance of every element touching the star nodes on the port nodes `k` and star nodes `s` (a lumped element by its stamp, a power conversion element by its nominal admittance, decision D14), eliminate `Y_red = Y_kk - Y_ks Y_ss⁻¹ Y_sk` and take `Y1 = (1/3) Wᴴ Y_red W` on the port. The star voltages per unit port voltage are `ν = -Y_ss⁻¹ Y_sk W`.
2. Each element `e` touching a port (a carrier) has its own share `σ_e = (1/3) Δwᴴ Y_e Δw`, `Δw` the pattern voltages across its conductors (port phasor minus `ν`). For a single branch it is a positive real multiple of its own admittance.
3. When every element of the star touches a port, `Σ σ_e = Y1` exactly and each carrier writes its own share.
4. An element that touches no port (a neutral reactor, a load from the star to earth) is disabled, and the rest `R = Y1 - Σ σ_e` goes to the carriers so that each stays an element of its class:
   - If power conversion elements are among the carriers, they take `R` in proportion to their own shares by the complex factor `f_pc = 1 + R / Σ_pc σ_e`, and every other carrier keeps its own share. A power conversion element holds any complex power through `kw` and `kvar`. Accepted when each such carrier keeps the sign of its active power (`Re(f_pc σ_e)` of the sign of `Re σ_e`).
   - Otherwise every carrier is scaled by `f = Y1 / Σ σ_e`, which must be a positive real number within `1e-9` relative: a `cuf` Capacitor holds a positive multiple of `jω`, a Reactor a branch of its own `R/X` ratio.
   - Else the command stops (decision D15).
5. Each carrier writes its final share in its class's own terms (section 1.13): a `cuf` Capacitor its `cuf` per step, a `kvar` Capacitor or Reactor its `kvar'` times `f` at the leg-rule `kV'` with `r'` and `rp'` divided by `f` (a positive real `f` scales the branch admittance, so its impedance divides by `f`), an R + jX or matrix Reactor `r`, `x` (and `rp`) from its admittance, a power conversion element by section 1.13 with the factor of item 4.

An internal node that the star of a transformer winding reaches (a wye winding on `x.1.2.3.4` with `x.4` touched by the winding and by other elements) is no chain and no joint star: the winding fixes it at the star value of the transformer solve (section 1.2 step 2, 0 for a balanced three-leg winding). Every other element reads it at that value as a fixed voltage per unit port voltage (its `ν`), the reduced winding writes its star as earth, and an element whose conductors all end on such nodes or on earth (a grounding reactor from `x.4` to `x.0`) lands on node 0 and is disabled (section 1.11).

Own derivation. On the deck `load.l2 phases=2 bus1=b.2.3.4 kw=600 pf=0.9 model=2` with `reactor.rn bus1=b.4 bus2=b.0 x=500` on a stiff 12.47 kV bus: `ν = -0.46035 - j0.06034`, `f_pc = 1.01935 - j0.03995`, `|Δw|` 0.80666 and 0.92721 on the legs to `b.2` and `b.3`, and the load writes `kv 7.76475` (`7.19956 / 0.92721`, section 1.13), `kw 175.688`, `kvar 93.754`, the exact share of the balanced state (453.127 + j241.807 kVA before and after, recipe R-3). On `reactor.rs1 bus1=b.1 bus2=x.1 r=1 x=10` with `capacitor.cx bus1=x.1 bus2=b.2 cuf=4` both elements touch a port, `ν = 1.02431 + j0.01093`, and they write `r 4223.764`, `x 42237.641` and `cuf 4.12341`, exact. Adding `load.lx bus1=x.1 kv=7.2 kw=100 pf=0.88` (touching no port, at the class's default power factor) gives `f = 0.76539 - j0.43510` with no power conversion carrier, so the command stops (at unity power factor `f = 1.00055 - j0.44347`).

Books for the chain rule (K2): Tleis section 3.3.4, eq. (3.120a) to (3.122), pp. 158 to 160, and Example 3.5, pp. 170 to 173 (the sections are summed along the continuous sheath conductors and `VS = 0` is imposed once at the ends of the major section), CIGRE TB 531 (WG B1.30, 2013), section 2.2.2, p. 12 (the screen currents determined "as a function of the currents flowing in the phase conductors, taking into account the screen bonding technique"), section 3.3, pp. 25, 26, and section 4.2.4.3, Table 10, p. 52 (positive-sequence impedance of a cross-bonded link `Zd = Za - Zx`, no screen current, against `Za - Zx - (Zm - Zx)²/(Zs - Zx)` for solid bonding), Дмитриев, Заземление экранов однофазных силовых кабелей, table 3.5 note 3, p. 37, section 7.1, p. 85, with eq. (7.1) to (7.4) on p. 86 and table 7.2 on p. 87, IEC 60287-1-1:2006+AMD1:2014, clause 2.3.6.2, p. 22 (the circulating loss of a major section with minor sections `a`, `pa`, `qa` is the solid-bonding loss times `(p² + q² - pq - p - q + 1)/(p + q + 1)²`, `1/16` for 0.5, 0.25, 0.25 km, the `|K_T|² = 0.0625` of Дмитриев), Clarke Vol. II p. 74 (b), Ametani et al., Power System Transients: Theory and Applications, 2nd ed. (2016), section 3.2.3.1, eq. (3.14), Hase p. 550 and section 23.5.1, pp. 559, 560 (the cross-bonded sheath circuits balanced "over the total length of the three spans"). Solid bonding as the Kron reduction of the sheaths: IEC TR 60909-2:2008, Table 7, eq. (15), p. 22, Wagner and Evans, Symmetrical Components (1933), p. 200, eq. (233) (the sheath loss "represented by correspondingly increasing the value of resistance assigned to the conductor"), Kersting 3rd ed. section 4.2.1, p. 101 (the concentric neutrals numbered and Kron-reduced) and section 5.6, p. 134 (no electric field beyond a screen, the strike of `C`). The C reading of a chain, a star of several elements with its class-aware scale, a winding star read at its solved value and a tap inside a major section are own derivation.

### 1.7 Merge of parallel conductors

Parallel conductors between the same two port nodes merge before the projection, on the matrices after sections 1.5 and 1.6:
- `Zb = (Aᵀ Z⁻¹ A)⁻¹` (equal drop, currents add),
- `Cb = Aᵀ C A` (equal potential, charges add),
- the core currents under a balanced phase current `i` of the merged conductor: `I = Z⁻¹ A Zb i` (used by the rating, section 1.10).

Books: Dommel section 4.1.2.2, method 1, pp. 4-19 to 4-21 ("it can easily handle the unequal current distribution in asymmetrical bundles"), Kersting eq. (6.89), p. 171, with Example 6.9, pp. 171, 172, Anderson section 4.9, eq. (4.154) to (4.160), pp. 107, 108, Grainger section 4.8, p. 155. `Cb = Aᵀ C A` follows Dommel's bundling conditions as well (section 4.1.2, p. 4-18: the currents add and `dV/dx` is equal in (4.2), the charges add and `v1 = v2` in (4.19), and section 4.1.2.2, method 1, p. 4-20: "the matrix reduction process is the same for [Z'] and [P']", with `[C']` the inverse of the reduced `[P']` as section 4.1.2.1, p. 4-19, states it for ground wires), since reducing `P` under equal potentials gives `P_b = (Aᵀ P⁻¹ A)⁻¹`, that is `Cb = Aᵀ C A`. Merging first is required: projecting each group of conductors alone and putting the results in parallel drops the mutual between the groups and the unequal sharing (on the nine-core deck of MPS-04 the uncoupled triples in parallel miss the source power by 7.3e-5 where the merge keeps it to 2.8e-9, and even the coupled three-circuit element with both ends tied misses it by 2.1e-5).

### 1.8 Projection in two forms

A coupled set whose conductors all join port nodes of equal phasor takes the impedance form `Z1 = 3 G⁻¹ (Wᴴ Zb W) G⁻¹`, `G = Wᴴ W`. Every other set, and every `cmatrix` of a Line, takes the admittance form `Y1 = (1/3) Wᴴ Y W` on its nodal matrix. Entry by entry: `Z1[c,d] = (3/(N_c N_d)) w_cᴴ Zb w_d` and `Y1[j,k] = (1/3) w_jᴴ Y w_k`.

The form rule is static: it reads the node map, never the solved state (stated limit L7). For a lumped element the classification is:
- every conductor joins a port node at terminal 1 to a port node of equal phasor at terminal 2: series, impedance form on `Z = Y_branch⁻¹`,
- every conductor ends on earth, on the element's own star or on a port node of the same port at terminal 2 (a branch between phases): shunt, admittance form on the nodal matrix,
- some conductors series and others shunt: refused (case 5),
- a conductor between port nodes of different ports and different phasors: refused (decision D15).

A Line's series part always takes the impedance form (its conductors join equal phasors, else case 1 or 4), its `C` always the admittance form, `C1 = (1/3) Wᴴ Cb W` lumped half at each end as the Line stamps it. The total `Z` of a Line is `z · len / units_convert` (its `length_multiplier`) for the matrix and symmetrical-component forms and as stored for geometry and spacing, `C` likewise from `Im(yc)/ω`, `ω = 2π base_frequency`.

| instance | impedance form | admittance form | book |
|---|---|---|---|
| three phases `(1, a², a)` | `S(Z) = (1/3) vᴴ Z v` | `S(Y)` | Dommel eq. (4.34), p. 4-24, Tleis eq. (2.26a), p. 38, eq. (4.61b), p. 274, Kersting eq. (4.68), Clarke Vol. I eq. [7], p. 229 (impedance), Kersting eq. (5.36), pp. 133, 134, Tleis eq. (3.39a), (3.41), p. 95 (admittance) |
| two phases 120 degrees apart, symmetric block | `(3/4)(Z11 + Z22 - Z12)` | `(C11 + C22 - C12)/3` | own derivation (Clarke Vol. I eq. [47], p. 146, which puts the network's `z2 z0/(z2 + z0)` across an opening, gives this value at first order when the line's own sequence impedances stand for the network: an analogy, not a derivation) |
| two poles `(1, -1)` | `(3/4)(Z11 + Z22 - 2 Z12)` | `(1/3)(Y11 + Y22 - 2 Y12)` | Clarke Vol. I p. 289 ("one-half the impedance of the loop"), p. 293 eq. [23], [24], Dommel eq. (4.63), Kersting section 11.1. The base factor `3/2` is own derivation |
| one phase | `3 Z` | `Y/3`, `C/3` | Clarke Vol. I eq. [100], [101], p. 426 (`3 Z`). `C/3` is own derivation |
| one branch between two phases of one port | | `(1/3) |w1 - w2|² y`, which is `y` for 120 degrees | own derivation (the books write `z + Z2`, Tleis eq. (2.59a), (2.60), p. 51) |
| closed delta ring of leg admittance matrix `Yb` | | `3 S(Yb)` | Tleis eq. (3.138a), (3.138c), p. 167, Clarke Vol. I p. 451 |
| series unequal impedances | `(1/3) Σ z_k` for three uncoupled | | Grainger eq. (11.89) to (11.91), pp. 459 to 461 |

Three rules keep the three-phase reductions of today to the bit, so no oracle cell moves where the value does not:
- `S(·)` of a symmetric matrix keeps the arithmetic of today's `elements::pos_seq::pos_seq_self_term` (sum of the diagonal minus the sum of the symmetric pairs, divided by 3, in that order). A reciprocal element (Line, Reactor, Capacitor and Fault matrices and their inverses) is read on its symmetric part, which takes the last-bit asymmetry of a numerical inverse out. A stamp that is non-reciprocal by construction (a Vsource or a z1/z2/z0 Reactor with `Z2 ≠ Z1`) goes through `(1/3) vᴴ X v` in full.
- A three-phase element on one port in positive order whose given data are already its positive-sequence values writes them unchanged, since the projection of its balanced stamp equals them: a Line in symmetrical-component form (`r1`, `x1`, `c1`), a wye Reactor given by `r` and `x` or by `z1`/`z2`/`z0` (its `z1`), a wye `kvar` Capacitor or Reactor, a wye `cuf` Capacitor of equal phases, a Fault given by `r`, a three-phase Vsource (`r1`, `x1`). The projection of a balanced stamp equals these values in exact arithmetic, so writing them as given changes nothing but the last-bit noise of an inversion round trip.
- A three-phase matrix, geometry or spacing Line keeps the arithmetic of `pd::line::Line::make_pos_sequence` (the diagonal sum and the upper-pair sum of `z` each divided by `3 · length_mult`, subtracted, then divided by `units_convert`, and `c1 = (cs - cm) / (2π f) / (3 · length_mult) · 1e9 / units_convert`), which is `S(Z)` per unit length in exact arithmetic.

Any other `W` goes through the general formula.

### 1.9 Sources

A Vsource becomes `E1 = (1/N) wᴴ E` behind its impedance in the impedance form. An Isource becomes `(1/3) Wᴴ I`. `w` are the phasors the map gives the source's port (a seeding source gets its own pattern back).

- Vsource EMF: `E_k` per conductor exactly as `pc/vsource/source.rs` stamps it, `Vmag · pu · exp(j(angle - (k-1) 360/N))` for the positive sequence (`Vmag = kV/√3` for three phases, `kV/2` per pole for two, `kV` for one, by `get_vmag`), rotating the other way for the negative sequence and not at all for the zero sequence, so that a negative- or zero-sequence source projects to `E1 = 0`. Reduced: one phase, `basekv' = |E1| / 1000 / pu`, `angle' = arg(E1)` in degrees. Three phases at the seed: `basekv/√3`, angle kept. Two poles: the pole voltage `kV/2`. One phase: `kV` kept. A Vsource reached through a winding: `θ = arg w(x.1)` the map's phasor of its first node and `angle' = angle - θ`. Behind the default lagging delta-wye bank `θ = -30` degrees, so a source written with `angle=0` takes `angle' = 30`.
- Vsource impedance: `Z1' = 3 G⁻¹ (wᴴ Zs w) G⁻¹` on its primitive matrix `Zs` (the `Vsource::z` it stamps). Three phases: its given `r1`, `x1` (the positive-sequence eigenvalue of its stamp, also when `Z2 ≠ Z1`, by the kept-value rule of section 1.8). Two poles: `1.5 (Zs - Zm) = 1.5 Z1` (with `Zs = (2 Z1 + Z0)/3`, `Zm = (Z0 - Z1)/3`). One phase: `3 Zs`, which is `3 Z1` since a one-phase source stamps `Z1` (its recalc forces `Z0 = Z2 = Z1`). Written as `r1`, `x1` of the one-phase source (decision D19).
- Isource: `I1 = (1/3) Wᴴ I`, `I_k` the injection per conductor as the Isource stamps it. One phase: `I/3`. Two phases (the engine injects at 0 and -120 degrees): `2I/3` on its port. Three phases: `I`. Written as `amps' = |I1|`, `angle' = arg(I1)`, `phases=1`.
- GICLine and GICsource: their EMF is the same on every conductor (zero sequence, the GICLine has no `sequence` property), so it projects to `E1 = 0` and the reduced element writes `volts=0`. Their series impedance takes the impedance form.
- Book: a balanced set has a positive-sequence component only, Tleis Example 2.1, p. 40, Fortescue (1918) eq. (102) to (104), pp. 1062, 1063, Clarke Vol. I eq. [14], [15], p. 312. Two poles 180 degrees apart, Clarke Vol. I pp. 287 to 293, Dommel eq. (4.63), p. 4-44 (the normalised `(1/√2)[1, 1; 1, -1]`). The Isource rule and the base factor `3/N` are own derivation.

### 1.10 The element written

One element of the same class with `phases=M` and `M x M` matrices, `M` the number of circuits, so the inter-circuit mutual impedance and capacitance stay. `M = 1` is the scalar form.

- What is written: the symmetric part `(Z1 + Z1ᵀ)/2` as `rmatrix` (real part) and `xmatrix` (imaginary part), the symmetric real part of `C1` as `cmatrix`. A Line writes per unit of its own length in its own length units (ohm and nF per unit length), a Reactor in ohm, a Capacitor `cmatrix` in µF. `M = 1` writes the scalar properties: Line `r1`, `x1`, `c1`, Reactor `r`, `x` (and `rp` per decision D19), Capacitor `cuf` per step, Fault a one-entry `gmatrix` or `r` as it was given, GICTransformer its winding resistances. The antisymmetric part `A12` is not written (stated limit L1). Numbers are written by Rust's shortest round-trip formatting, so no digit is lost.
- Write order of a Line (its property side effects decide it): `phases=` is accepted only on a Line in symmetrical-component form with no geometry, and `r1`, `x1`, `c1` as well as `rmatrix`, `xmatrix`, `cmatrix` reset the length units. So `M = 1` writes `r1`, `x1`, `c1`, then `phases=1`, then `normamps`, `emergamps`, the seasonal `ratings`, then `units`, as today. `M > 1` writes `r1`, `x1` with the values of `Z1[1,1]` per unit length (they drop the geometry and make the Line a symmetrical-component Line, and the matrices written next supersede them), then `phases=M`, then `rmatrix`, `xmatrix`, `cmatrix`, then the ratings, then `units`, in one edit.
- Coupled writes of the other classes (`M > 1`), each the symmetric part of the projection in the class's own terms. `C1 = (1/3) Wᴴ C W` of a real symmetric `C` and `Y1` of a real symmetric `G` are Hermitian, so their symmetric part is their real part, and the imaginary part left out is the antisymmetric term of stated limit L1:
  - Reactor, series (impedance form): `rmatrix`, `xmatrix` the real and imaginary parts of `(Z1 + Z1ᵀ)/2`, `parallel=no`.
  - Reactor, shunt (admittance form): `rmatrix`, `xmatrix` the real and imaginary parts of `((Y1 + Y1ᵀ)/2)⁻¹`, `parallel=no`, `bus2` earth. The symmetric part is taken before the inversion.
  - Capacitor, shunt (admittance form on its nodal `C`): `cmatrix = Re(C1)` in µF. A bank with series `R` or `XL` takes them by `3/N_c` (section 1.13) and is refused, as the series bank below, when its circuits differ in `N_c`.
  - Capacitor, series (impedance form): `Z1 = zl' I + Zc1`, the series `zl = R + jXL` of each step by the factor `3/N_c` of its circuits and `Zc1` the projection of `(jωC)⁻¹`. `cmatrix = (jω (Zc1 + Zc1ᵀ)/2)⁻¹`, real and symmetric for a passive `C` (`Zc1 = -j ω⁻¹ H` with `H` Hermitian, so the inverse is `Re(H)⁻¹`), in µF. The class holds one `R` and one `XL` per step for all its phases, so a bank with `R` or `XL` whose circuits differ in `N_c` is refused (section 1.12).
  - Fault: `gmatrix = Re(Y1)`, `M x M`, which is positive semidefinite when `G` is.
  - GICTransformer: its winding resistances times `k` (section 1.13). The class holds one resistance per winding for all phases, so a GICTransformer whose circuits differ in `N_c` is refused (section 1.12).
- Ratings: a Line circuit of `N_c` phases scales `normamps` and `emergamps` by `N_c/3`. A merged bundle takes `rating = min_k (rating_k / share_k)` with `share_k = |I_k| / |i|` from section 1.7, then the factor `N_c/3`. The engine holds one `normamps` and one `emergamps` per Line (a geometry Line takes the first wire's), so every core has the Line's rating and the bundle rating is `rating / max_k share_k`. A rating that is not set stays not set (queue step 2), and so does a typed negative other than `-1`, which is stored as typed, reads as not set and raises no error (the rating details of queue step 10, section 5): the reduction leaves it as stored and raises no error for it either. Every other typed rating is rewritten typed. The seasonal `ratings` array, when given, scales entry by entry by the same factor. A coupled `phases=M` element holds one rating, so it writes the smallest of its circuits' ratings (stated limit L10). User-confirmed (decision D6). Own derivation (equal sharing is the textbook assumption, Grainger p. 155).
- Book: the `M x M` coupled element, Tleis eq. (3.146a), (3.147a), (3.147b), pp. 177 to 179, eq. (3.150a), p. 181, Example 3.4, pp. 135 to 140, Clarke Vol. I pp. 419 to 421, eq. [97], [98].

### 1.11 Buses

Port `j` of a bus becomes node `j`. Internal nodes and earth become node 0. An element left with every conductor on node 0 is disabled.

Written form (decision D12). Each terminal of each reduced element gets one reduced node per conductor of the reduced element:
- reduced phase conductor `c` (1 to `M`) takes the reduced node of the port that circuit `c` reaches at that terminal, or 0 when circuit `c` lands on earth or on internal nodes there,
- every other conductor of the reduced element (the neutral of a reduced wye Load, the second terminal's conductors of a shunt) takes 0,
- a lumped element whose port nodes all lie on one port and whose other conductors end on earth, on internal nodes or on that same port (a shunt by the map) is written from its port node at terminal 1 to earth at terminal 2, spelled on the port's bus (`<port bus>.0`), whatever the order of its original terminals and whatever bus its internal end lies on: a Capacitor from `b.1` to `b.2` becomes `bus1=b`, `bus2=b.0`, a Reactor from `b.1` to the internal node `x.1` becomes `bus1=b`, `bus2=b.0`, and a Capacitor from the internal node `x.1` to `b.2` becomes `bus1=b`, `bus2=b.0`.

So a Load between two phases on `c.1.2` becomes `c` (its conductor 2 on 0), a delta Load on `b1.1.2.3` becomes `b1`, and no reduced element is shorted. When the list equals the default of the reduced element (nodes `1..M` for its phase conductors, 0 for the rest) the bus is written bare (`b`), which keeps today's spelling for the common case. Otherwise it is written explicitly (`b.0`, `b.2`, `b.1.2`, `b.1.1`).

The write goes through the bus properties of the class by the property edit path, so `BusNameRedefined` is raised as for any edit: `bus1` and `bus2` (Line, Reactor, Capacitor, Fault, Isource, Vsource, GICLine, UPFC), `buses` (Transformer, AutoTrans, all windings at once), `BusH`, `BusNH`, `BusX`, `BusNX` (GICTransformer), `bus1` alone for the one-terminal classes. A control or meter has no bus of its own: it takes the bus of its reduced target through `PosSeqElemInfo::bus_names`, filled after the target is reduced (decision D16), so the map writes no bus for it. Decision D22 rescales the settings it compares with a voltage or a current read through the target and keeps those it compares with a power, which the engine reads at the balanced total.

State kept (decision D9): a reduced element keeps its enabled state, and after its writes the applier opens conductor `c` of terminal `t` of the reduced element when circuit `c` was open at terminal `t` (every phase of the circuit open there), since `phases=` reallocates the terminals closed.

### 1.12 Refusals and the unchanged circuit

One criterion: the balanced pattern cannot be written, or the result is not one reciprocal passive element of the class (a Line of a chain holds one of several shares of a passive total, section 1.6 item 9, so the criterion reads on the chain). A refusal stops the command with the element named and changes nothing (no element, bus, property or circuit flag moves). A multi-phase element is never left on a reduced circuit. Own derivation, bar the Fault (IEEE Std 551-2006 section 3.6.12, p. 61: "An unbalanced fault condition is the most common circuit condition that invalidates the single-phase (or positive sequence) equivalent circuit condition method", Tleis eq. (2.47), p. 48, eq. (2.60), p. 51).

The message is the contract and every pin asserts it in full. Angles print in degrees with one decimal in `[0, 360)`. The form is `MakePosSequence: <what>. Nothing was changed.`, sentences joined by one space. The command pushes it as `DssDiagnostic::msg(text, None)`: no upstream error number exists for these refusals, and every refusal pin asserts the code `None` as well:

| case | trigger | message (example values) | step |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | two different phasors reach one node through Lines, `Through` legs or windings | `MakePosSequence: node c.1 receives two phasors (0.0 and 120.0 degrees) through Line.l1 and Line.l2. Nothing was changed.` | MPS-02 |
| 2 | a multi-phase terminal of an element whose model depends on the order does not see its own pattern on its port (section 1.4 item 3) | `MakePosSequence: IndMach012.mn has its phases on c.1.3.2, which is not one port in positive order. Nothing was changed.` | MPS-02 |
| 2 | a Vsource or Isource of more than three phases | `MakePosSequence: Vsource.v4 has 4 phases. A source of more than three phases has no positive-sequence pattern. Nothing was changed.` (`Isource.i4 has 4 phases.` the same) | MPS-02 |
| 3 | a group, as it forms or at a join, holds one phasor twice or phasors that are not one balanced set (section 1.4 items 1 and 2) | at a join `MakePosSequence: Vsource.source and Reactor.r14 group the phase nodes of bus b differently. Nothing was changed.`, for a lone group `MakePosSequence: Load.l4 has its phases on d.1.2.3.4, which is not one balanced set. Nothing was changed.` | MPS-02 |
| 4 | a Line conductor joins a port node to earth, or two phase nodes of one bus | `MakePosSequence: conductor 3 of Line.l joins b.3 to c.0, a node of another phasor. Nothing was changed.` | MPS-02 |
| 5 | a lumped element mixes series and shunt conductors | `MakePosSequence: Reactor.r joins some conductors between ports and others to earth. Nothing was changed.` | MPS-03 |
| 6 | a matrix whose order is not the phase count, and every other reason the element's own solve refuses it (every class whose solve can refuse implements `pos_seq_refusal`: Reactor, Capacitor, Fault and Line today) | `MakePosSequence: ` + the element's solve message without its trailing ` Aborting solution.` + ` Nothing was changed.`, for example `MakePosSequence: Reactor.rw has 2 phases but its rmatrix has 3 x 3 entries. Specify rmatrix and xmatrix for 2 phases. Nothing was changed.` (the text of `pd::matrix_order_refusal`) and `MakePosSequence: Matrix Inversion Error for Line "ls". Invalid impedance specified. Nothing was changed.` (the Line's singular series impedance) | MPS-01 |
| 7 | a `gmatrix` whose symmetric part is not positive semidefinite, or a negative `r` | `MakePosSequence: the gmatrix of Fault.f is not passive. Nothing was changed.` (`the r of` for a fault given by `r`) | MPS-01 |
| 8 | a one- or two-phase Fault | `MakePosSequence: Fault.f has 2 phases. A one- or two-phase fault has no positive-sequence form. Nothing was changed.` (`1 phase` in the singular) | MPS-01 |

Patterns the criterion covers that the eight cases do not name (decisions D9, D10, D15 and section 1.2 step 2), each with its message: a lumped conductor between ports of different phasor (`MakePosSequence: Reactor.r joins b.1 and c.2, ports of different phasors. Nothing was changed.`), a circuit open on some phases and closed on others at one terminal (`MakePosSequence: Line.l has conductor 2 open at terminal 2 while its circuit's other phases are closed. Nothing was changed.`), a transformer group whose rows leave a node without a value when the map is complete, while a leg of the group touches a node that carries one (section 1.2 step 2: `MakePosSequence: the windings of Transformer.ty leave node b.5 without a balanced voltage. Nothing was changed.`), a class whose one-phase form does not hold a third of its balanced power (`MakePosSequence: UPFC.u has no one-phase form. Nothing was changed.`), a joint star whose carrying elements cannot stay passive (`MakePosSequence: the star at b.4 cannot be written into Capacitor.c1, Capacitor.c2. Nothing was changed.`), a coupled element whose class holds one series value for circuits of different phase count (section 1.10: `MakePosSequence: Capacitor.c5 joins circuits of different phase count through one series R and XL. Nothing was changed.`, and `through one winding resistance` for a GICTransformer).

Passivity of a `gmatrix` (case 7): the symmetric part `(G + Gᵀ)/2` is positive semidefinite when its smallest eigenvalue is at least `-1e-12 · max_ij |G_ij|`. The eigenvalues come from cyclic Jacobi rotations on the real symmetric matrix until the off-diagonal norm falls below `1e-15` of the Frobenius norm (a few dozen lines in `elements/pos_seq/project.rs`). Own derivation (Anderson section 2.5, p. 30, for reciprocity and passivity).

Atomicity: the command analyses the whole circuit first (map, eliminations, every reduced value, every refusal) without editing anything, and only then applies all edits. `positive_sequence` is set only after the analysis succeeded. Which refusal is reported when several apply: the analysis runs its stages in this order and reports the first refusal it meets, so exactly one message is pushed:
1. the element checks (cases 6, 7, 8) over every circuit element in creation order,
2. the map (cases 1, 2, 3, 4, the D9 open circuit, the D15 lumped conductor between ports of different phasors, the undetermined winding) in the walk order of section 1.2,
3. the projection, the chains and joint stars, the sources, the power conversion elements and the controls (case 5, the coupled element its class cannot hold, the joint star, D10), element by element in creation order within each stage.

Every value the apply writes was produced and checked by the analysis, so an `Err` from the property edit path during the apply is a defect of the analysis: the applier stops there and pushes `MakePosSequence: <Class.name>.<property>=<value> was refused by the class: <its message>.`, and a `debug_assert!` makes every test that reaches it fail.

### 1.13 Power conversion elements and windings

An element becomes the positive-sequence self term of what it stamps on its real nodes. Its rated power divides by 3 for every phase count wherever its legs sit at one per-unit voltage in the balanced state, it carries its circuit's current alone and it shares no star with another element. Three cases write another power. An element on a joint star with others, a one-leg element included, writes the leg rule's power times `conj(f)` (section 1.6 item 4). An element whose legs sit at different per-unit voltages (a two-phase delta element, an element of unequal legs on a joint star) writes the nominal admittance's power at its largest leg's voltage by the leg rule below (decision D17, stated limit L11). A unit of a bank of one-phase units reduced in parallel between one pair of ports divides its rated power by `|Δw_r| / α_I` (the transformer bullets below), which is 3 for every unit that carries its circuit's current alone and `2√3` for a unit of an open-delta pair. For a power conversion element (Load, Generator, PVSystem, Storage, WindGen and the rest) and a `kvar`-rated Capacitor or Reactor the self term is the admittance form on the element's nominal admittance, written in the class's own rated terms. Own derivation (Clarke Vol. I eq. [72], Kersting eq. (5.36) for three phases).

- Leg rule (decision D17). The reduced element sits from its port node to earth at `kV' = V_leg,r / |Δw_r|`, `r` the leg of the largest per-unit voltage `|Δw_k| / V_leg,k` in the balanced state (the first in conductor order on ties). Its written power is
  `S' = conj(f) · (1/3) Σ_k (|Δw_k| · kV' / V_leg,k)² · S_leg,k`,
  `f = 1` outside a joint star (section 1.6 item 4). Every other rated quantity of the class (kVA, `xfkva`, `kWh`, `kwhrated`, `kwhstored`, `pmpp`, `kvarmax`, `kvarmaxabs`, `minkvar`, `maxkvar`, `prated`) divides by 3, since the engine compares each with the element's own power (the Storage's state of charge with its own `dckw`, for example), and percentages, per-unit data and `pf` stay. A rated quantity the engine compares with a balanced-total reading stays: the Generator's `FuelkWh`, whose fuel check (`elements::pc::generator::Generator::take_sample`) burns the power its registers sample, which it multiplies by 3 on a positive-sequence circuit, so the unreduced tank empties at the unreduced rate. The admittance the element is written with, `conj(S') / kV'²`, does not depend on the choice of `r`, only its rated voltage does.
  - When all legs sit at one per-unit voltage (every three-phase wye or delta element, a one-phase element to earth or between two phases of one port, a two-phase wye element, an element between two poles, and an element of equal legs on its own star), each term of the sum is 1, so `S'` is the rated power over 3 at `kV' = V_leg/|Δw|`: the reduced element sits at the per-unit voltage its legs sit at, and the reduction is exact for every load model and its `vminpu` switch. This gives `kV/√3` for a three-phase wye or delta element, `kV` for a one-phase element to earth, `kV/√3` for a one-phase element between two phases (`V_leg = kV`, `|Δw| = √3`), `kV/2` between two poles, and for a two-leg element on its own star (`|Δw| = √3/2` on each leg) `(kV/√3)/(√3/2) = 2 kV/3`, 8.31333 kV for 12.47 kV, with the rated power over 3.
  - Legs at different per-unit voltages (a two-phase delta element, whose second leg runs to earth at `1/√3` of the first leg's per-unit voltage, an element on a joint star with other elements) make `S'` the nominal admittance's power at the voltage of the largest leg: exact for constant impedance and for a `kvar` Capacitor or Reactor, off for another load model by the voltages its legs leave (stated limit L11). Here the written power is not the rated power over 3 (`kw 133.333` for a two-phase delta Load of 600 kW), and decision D17 gives the candidate that keeps it and why the plan does not take it.
- Projection factor of a `kvar` Reactor or Capacitor and of the GICTransformer resistances: `k = 3 / Σ_k |Δw_k|²`, the ratio of the reduced branch impedance to the leg impedance for equal legs. The reduced `kvar` element writes `kvar'` and `kV'` by the leg rule and `r' = k r`, `rp' = k rp` (`k = 1` for a three-phase wye element, `1/3` for a closed delta, `3` for one phase to earth, `3/2` for two phases to earth, `3/4` for the two-phase delta element with one leg to earth), so `X' = kV'² / kvar'` equals `k X_leg`.
- The Capacitor's series `R` and `XL` of each step scale with the path they sit on in its stamp (`Capacitor::make_yprim_work`). A wye, one- or two-phase bank given by `kvar` or `cuf` folds `zl = R + jXL` into each leg, so the whole series branch scales by `k`: `R' = k R`, `XL' = k XL` per step (`3` for one phase to earth, `3/2` for two phases to earth, `1` for three phases). A delta bank and a `cmatrix` bank add `zl` in series at each phase node (on the diagonal of the inverted nodal matrix), so it scales by `3/N_c` of the phase-node path, which keeps it for three phases. Own derivation (the same projection as the capacitance it sits in series with). The exact stamp of that path is `Y = Y_d (I + zl Y_d)⁻¹` on the nodal matrix `Y_d` of the bank: on a closed three-phase delta of leg admittance `y` its positive-sequence value is `3y / (1 + 3y zl)`, a wye leg of `3y` in series with `zl`, so `R` and `XL` are kept exactly. The engine stamps that arm with the leg diagonal scaled by 1.000001 before it inverts, and both oracles stamp it the same way: that adds a shunt of `2e-6` of the leg admittance on each node, so the unreduced bank draws 6.7e-7 more than physics and gains a zero-sequence shunt of 1.29e-9 S (100 kvar a leg at 12.47 kV, own derivation), until queue step 20 puts the exact form in its place with a pin against the wye equivalent, since no corpus deck reaches the arm. The MPS-03 pin `a_delta_capacitor_with_a_series_reactor_keeps_it` holds the written values at T1 and its balance at T3 once step 20 has landed, below `1e-5` with the value recorded (T6) before.
- Transformer: `phases=1`, every winding wye from its port node to earth, `kV'` per winding by the leg rule on its `V_leg` and `Δw`, `kVA'` of every winding and `NormHkVA`, `EmergHkVA` divided by 3, `%R`, `%X` kept (at `kVA/3` and `kV/√3` the ohmic impedance equals the unit's own). A one-phase transformer on any phase is kept, not disabled. The displacement is in `W`, so the reduced transformer has none. This is the reduction of a three-phase bank and of a one-phase unit alone between its two ports, each of which carries its circuit's current.
- A bank of one-phase units between one pair of ports (three units into a delta, an open-delta pair), with every one-phase Line conductor and lumped branch that joins the same two ports (the jumper of an open-delta regulator bank, `Line.Jumper` of the IEEE 37 feeder between the common phase of its two ports), is one set, projected jointly with a positive-sequence current imposed at the far port: each unit is its ideal transformer (ratio and tap) with its series impedance, each conductor its impedance, the current law holds at every node of the far port, and the far port's currents are `I1 v`. The set's series impedance is `Z_set = (1/3) vᴴ Δ / I1` with `Δ` the drop from port to port, its ratio the units' common ratio. Each unit `k` writes `kV'` by the leg rule, `kVA'_k = α_I,k S_k / |Δw_r|` with `α_I,k` its reduced current (its share of `I1` by the parallel of the units' ohmic impedances) over its real current in the solve, and `%R`, `%X` from the ohmic impedance `z_k Z_set / Z_par`, `Z_par` the parallel of the units' ohmic impedances referred to the far side. A conductor of the set that carries no current in the solve is disabled. For a balanced bank the solve gives every unit `kVA/3` with `%R`, `%X` kept, so the bullet above stands. For an open-delta pair of equal units it gives `Z_set = 2z/3`, each unit at `4z/3`: `kVA/(2√3)` with `%R`, `%X` times `2/√3`, `α_I = 1/2`, and the jumper disabled. Own derivation: with `Δ_1 - Δ_2 = z I1` and `Δ_3 - Δ_2 = z a I1`, `(1/3) vᴴ Δ = (2z/3) I1` whatever `Δ_2`, so a conductor on the common phase carries `I1 (1 + a² + a) = 0` and enters nothing. Each real unit carries a line current (Kersting eq. (8.104), p. 281: `I_ba = I_a`, `I_cb = -I_c`), so the bank's balanced capacity is `√3 S_u`, which `kVA/(2√3)` per unit keeps as the loading reading, where `kVA/3` would read the units' loading `√3/2` of the real one. Two units at `kVA/3` with `%R`, `%X` kept, in parallel at `z/2`, miss the source power of the open-delta deck of section 1.16 by 1.1e-2 where `2z/3` misses it by 4.7e-4, and with the IEEE 37 jumper at its `3 Z` they short the regulators (section 1.16). The open-delta bank is not balanced, and its own negative-sequence response under load is stated limit L12.
- AutoTrans: its series winding has `λ = 0` (both ends carry one phasor), so the leg rule reads its terminals: `phases=1`, `kV'` of each terminal (H and X) its rated line voltage over `√3` for a two- or three-phase bank and its rated voltage for one phase, `kVA'` and `NormHkVA`, `EmergHkVA` divided by 3, the series and common windings kept as series and wye, a tertiary written wye by the leg rule, `%R`, `%X` kept.
- A power conversion element's own star (a node only its conductors touch) is eliminated inside it by its nominal admittance (decision D14), `ν` as in section 1.5.

### 1.14 Instances of the procedure

| instance | where it lives in the procedure |
|---|---|
| the self term on the true phasors, rated power over 3 | the admittance form with `W` from the map (`v` are the true phasors, not `(1, a², a)` by node number), and section 1.13. Rated power over 3 holds for every element whose legs sit at one per-unit voltage in the balanced state (every three-phase element, every element to earth, between two phases or between two poles, and an element of equal legs on its own star), carries its circuit's current alone and shares no star with another element. An element on a joint star with others, a one-leg element included, writes the leg rule's power times `conj(f)` (section 1.6 item 4). For legs at different per-unit voltages (a two-phase delta element, an element of unequal legs on a joint star) the leg rule keeps the admittance and writes the power at the largest leg's voltage (decision D17), stated limit L11. A unit of a bank reduced in parallel between one pair of ports divides its rated power by `|Δw_r| / α_I`, `2√3` for an open-delta pair, so that its loading reads as the real unit's (section 1.13) |
| one phase `3 Z` and `C/3`, the two-phase Line, a series element in the impedance form | the instances of section 1.8 (one phase `3 Z` and `C/3`, two phases `(3/4)(Z11 + Z22 - Z12)`), the form rule by the map. The two-phase value is the impedance form of the series rule of section 1.8, the excitation by balanced currents `(1, a) i`: balanced voltages on the same block would give `3 (Z11 Z22 - Z12²) / (Z11 + Z22 + Z12)`, `0.524944 + j1.186838` against `0.574145 + j1.318688` ohm/mile on the lateral of section 1.16, 9.8 per cent apart |
| transposition sections and a shared neutral | carried by the procedure, not refused. Transposition sections are carried by the map (rolls, section 1.2), a shared neutral is an internal node (sections 1.5 and 1.6), and the nine-core deck on nodes `1.2.3.1.2.3.1.2.3` stays the pin of the merge (section 1.7, MPS-04) |
| a Line above three conductors | its conductors take their meaning from their nodes (sections 1.2 to 1.5): a neutral on node 0 at both ends is Kron-reduced in `Z` and struck in `C`, parallel cores merge (section 1.7) and further circuits are written with `phases=M` (section 1.10). The mean of the diagonal minus the mean over all pairs is the three-phase instance `S(Z)` alone (section 1.8) and is never taken over more than three conductors (decision D21, section 1.16) |
| the delta Reactor matrix of legs, the nodal delta `cmatrix` | a delta Reactor matrix holds leg quantities, stamped as a ring (MPS-07), reduced by the admittance form, `3 S(Yb)` for a closed ring. A delta Capacitor `cmatrix` is nodal for `N >= 3`, `C1 = S(C)`, and refused for `N = 1, 2` (decision D4) |
| a Reactor given one of `rmatrix`, `xmatrix` | the other part is a zero matrix, no message: an input rule of the Reactor (MPS-07 item 5) |
| the two-pole Vsource, the one- and two-phase Fault, a non-passive `gmatrix` | section 1.9 (two poles 180 degrees apart, one phase of the pole voltage behind `1.5 (Zs - Zm)`), refusal cases 7 and 8 of section 1.12 (case 8 by decision D2) |
| ratings | section 1.10 (`N_c/3` for a circuit of `N_c` phases, merged cores at the smallest of rating over current share, decision D6) |
| a matrix whose order is not the phase count | refusal at the solve as today, case 6 at the command. Dump, `?` and Save print it at its stored order |
| the repeat guard, the one-phase Vsource | the guard (decision D8): on a circuit already reduced the command changes nothing, so a reduced `phases=2` coupled element is never read as a two-phase lateral (MPS-04 item 5). The one-phase Vsource of section 1.9 keeps its `kV`, and its impedance takes the one-phase `3 Z` |
