| the conductor count of a delta Capacitor or Reactor | `N + 1` conductors for `N = 1, 2` and `N` for `N >= 3`, in either order of `conn=` and `phases=`: an input rule of the Capacitor and Reactor (MPS-07 item 2) |

### 1.15 Literature check

This section is not a rule to pin. It sorts the procedure into what the books confirm and what is this project's own derivation. Every committed doc comment and record follows this sorting.

| plan section and instance | books confirm | own derivation |
|---|---|---|
| 1.1 principle | the balanced state as the defining condition (Dommel eq. (4.34), Tleis eq. (2.26a)), power invariance under a current transformation (Anderson 8.5, Grainger 8.6), the amplitude-invariant transformation and its factor 3 (Tleis eq. (2.13), (2.18), Fortescue eq. (112)) | the reporting factor, the admittance-form dual with a general `W`, "no class has a rule of its own" |
| 1.2 node meaning and phasors | the books leave the shift out and warn about loops (Grainger p. 68), the neutral as the reference of the positive-sequence network (Grainger p. 462, Clarke Vol. I p. 312), the autotransformer's electrically connected windings in star (IEC 60076-8 3.1, 3.4, Grainger 2.7), a one-phase unit at clock 0 or 6 (IEC 60076-1 7.2.2) | the displacement carried in `W`, the source angle left out, the angle of an inter-circuit mutual across a displacement, the joint solve of windings with its zero-mean and star rows, the wait of a group no row determines |
| 1.3 lumped conductors | none | all, the rounds of the lumped stage |
| 1.4 ports | conductor order three by three as a convention (Dommel 4.1.4, Kersting eq. (4.83), Anderson eq. (4.265)), a static element's impedance the same in either order (Tleis p. 214, Grainger p. 461) | binding by terminals first, a group closed at one balanced set and checked as it forms, the join of groups with its balanced-set check, numbering by the lowest node, the order check by the element's own pattern |
| 1.5 internal nodes inside one element | earthed: Kron in `Z`, struck in `C` (Dommel 4.1.2.1, Kersting eq. (4.55), (5.13), Hase eq. (23.5), Clarke Vol. I p. 421). No closed path: struck in `Z` (Tleis p. 160, Clarke Vol. II p. 74). Earthed at one end: struck in `Z` and `C` (Dommel p. 4-30, Hase eq. (23.4)). Star of one element (Clarke Vol. I p. 275) | a floating conductor in `C`, a star of several elements and its class-aware scale |
| 1.6 chains and joint stars | K2 (Tleis 3.3.4 and Example 3.5, CIGRE TB 531 2.2.2, 3.3 and Table 10, Дмитриев 7.1, IEC 60287-1-1 2.3.6.2, Clarke Vol. II p. 74, Ametani 2nd ed. 3.2.3.1, Hase 23.5.1), solid bonding as the Kron reduction (IEC TR 60909-2 Table 7, Wagner and Evans eq. (233), Hase eq. (23.5)) | the `C` reading of a chain, a tap inside a major section, the negative share of a short minor section |
| 1.7 merge of parallel conductors | `Zb` (Dommel method 1, Kersting eq. (6.89), Anderson section 4.9, eq. (4.154) to (4.160)), `Cb = Aᵀ C A` (Dommel 4.1.2, p. 4-18, and 4.1.2.2 method 1, p. 4-20, `[P']` reduced under equal potentials and inverted) | none |
| 1.8 projection in two forms | three phases, two poles, one phase `3 Z`, shunt sets in the admittance form, the unit currents of an open-delta bank (Kersting eq. (8.104)) | two phases at 120 degrees, the base factor `3/N`, `C/3`, the static form rule, the joint projection of a bank between one pair of ports with its `2z/3` |
| 1.9 sources | the Vsource (Tleis Example 2.1, Fortescue eq. (102) to (104), Clarke Vol. I eq. [14], [15]) | the Isource, the base factor of the source impedance |
| 1.10 the element written | the `M x M` element (Tleis Example 3.4, Clarke pp. 419 to 421) | none |
| 1.11 buses, 1.12 the unchanged circuit | none | all |
| 1.12 refusals | the Fault refusal (IEEE Std 551 p. 61, Tleis eq. (2.47), (2.60)) | the other cases, the passivity test, every message |
| section 6, stated limits L1 to L6 | the symmetric part as the books' value (Clarke eq. [98], Tleis p. 140), the sequence mutual of two circuits not reciprocal in general and not for a mirrored order (Clarke Vol. I p. 420, Tleis Example 3.4, p. 139), the shunt impedance of a one-phase element off by `(Z2 + Z0) / (3z)` (Clarke Vol. I p. 291, p. 426, Tleis eq. (2.47)), unbalanced loads (Grainger eq. (11.91), pp. 460, 461) | the exactness condition `B13 - B31 = (B12 - B21) + (B23 - B32)` and the displacement rule `ρ = |tan θ|`, the lost reactive power `6 Re(A12) Im(conj(i1) i2)` with its active part `6 Im(A12) Im(conj(i1) i2)`, the source-power size of the one-phase element, the sizes of the fault-duty limit (1 to 4 per cent) and of the rolled-phase case (Kersting Example 4.2), the load between two phases (equal to IEEE Std 551 section 3.7, p. 68, "one conductor (one phase to neutral, NOT the round loop impedance)" with the result times `√3/2`, for uncoupled conductors, the mutual term own derivation) |
| 1.13 the self term on the true phasors, rated power over 3 | three phases (Kersting eq. (5.36), Tleis eq. (4.62a)), the unit currents of an open-delta bank (Kersting eq. (8.104), p. 281), from which its `√3 S_u` capacity follows | rated power divided by 3 for every phase count, the leg rule, the current share `α_I` of a unit in a bank between one pair of ports |
| 1.8 one phase `3 Z` and `C/3`, the two-phase Line, a series element in the impedance form | one phase `3 Z` (Clarke eq. [101]), series Reactor in the impedance form (Tleis eq. (4.61b)) | the two-phase Line value |
| 1.14 the delta Reactor matrix of legs, the nodal delta `cmatrix` | the nodal formula (Tleis eq. (3.138a), (3.138c), Clarke Vol. I p. 451) | the reading of a delta matrix (a language question) |
| 1.9 the two-pole Vsource, 1.12 case 8 the one- and two-phase Fault | the two-pole source (Clarke pp. 287 to 293, Dommel eq. (4.63)), the Fault refusal | the base factor `3/2` of the two-pole source |
| 1.10 ratings | none (equal sharing is the textbook assumption, Grainger p. 155) | `N/3` and the smallest of rating over share |

### 1.16 Recipes and reference numbers

The numbers below were re-derived on 2026-10-10 with numpy from the printed book data, with the recipes stated here. An executor re-derives every number it pins by the same recipe (in the test where the arithmetic is short, else in a numpy script of its own whose recipe goes into the pin's doc comment), never from this table alone.

Recipes:
- R-1 Modified Carson (Kersting eq. (4.41), (4.42), pp. 83, 84, third edition): `z_ii = r_i + 0.09530 + j0.12134 (ln(1/GMR_i) + 7.93402)`, `z_ij = 0.09530 + j0.12134 (ln(1/D_ij) + 7.93402)` ohm/mile, 60 Hz, 100 ohm m, distances in ft, positions as complex numbers `x + j h`.
- R-2 Potential coefficients (Kersting eq. (5.16), p. 125): `P_ii = 11.17689 ln(S_ii/RD_i)`, `P_ij = 11.17689 ln(S_ij/D_ij)` mile/µF, `S_ij = |d_i - conj(d_j)|`, `C = P⁻¹` (nodal, µF/mile), `ω = 376.9911`. Positions are absolute here (height above earth): Kersting Example 5.1 (pp. 123, 124) puts phases a, b, c at `0 + j29`, `2.5 + j29`, `7 + j29` ft and the neutral at `4 + j25` ft (the book: neutral 25 ft above ground, image distance `S_aa = 58` ft), radii `RD = 0.03004` ft for the phases (336,400 26/7 ACSR, 0.721 in diameter over 24) and `0.02346` ft for the neutral (4/0 6/1 ACSR). A radius is the diameter in inches over 24.
- R-3 Nodal reference solve: an ideal source fixes the source nodes at `V_ph v` (`V_ph = 12470/√3` V unless stated). A source with impedance is an ideal EMF behind its 3 x 3 `Zs = A diag(Z0, Z1, Z2) A⁻¹`, and its power is read at its terminal. Each Line is the block `Z⁻¹` between its end nodes plus `jω C/2` at each end. A load "P at pf" draws P in kW and `P tan(arccos pf)` in kvar. A constant-impedance load leg is `y = conj(S_leg) / V_leg²` at its rated leg voltage. A constant-power leg iterates `I = conj(S/V)` to a fixed point and has no switch to constant impedance, so every deck with `model=1` loads that a T5 pin compares with R-3 writes `vminpu=0.6` on them (the lowest solved voltage of the decks here is 0.913 pu). A matrix Line keeps a default capacitance unless `cmatrix` is given, so a deck whose numbers are computed without capacitance writes a `cmatrix` of zeros. Earth rows and columns are struck, the free node voltages solve `Y_ff V_f = -Y_fs V_s`, and the source power is `Σ V_s conj(I_s)` with `I = Y V`. The reduced circuit is solved the same way on one phase and reported times 3.
- R-4 The projection of section 1.1, the eliminations of section 1.5, the chain of section 1.6, the merge of section 1.7.
- R-5 Tleis Example 3.5 cross-bonding: from the printed 6 x 6 matrix (p. 172: core self `0.066 + j0.632`, sheath self `0.338 + j0.5814`, core to own sheath `0.0493 + j0.582`, any two conductors of adjacent cables `0.0493 + j0.508`, of the outer cables `0.0493 + j0.465` ohm/km, cores 1 to 3 then sheaths 4 to 6, flat formation), build one matrix per minor section `k = 0, 1, 2` with the core of phase `p` at position `(p + k) mod 3` and the sheaths fixed, solve the chain of section 1.6 with the sheaths earthed at both ends of the major section, take each section's `Z1_k`. The capacitances: core to own sheath `121.091 µS/km`, sheath to earth `864.191 µS/km` (p. 171), as nodal `C` the susceptance over `2π 50`. Page 172 prints the mutuals twice, with four decimals in the derivation (`j0.5822`, `j0.5086`, `j0.4651`) and with three in the 6 x 6. The three-decimal matrix gives the numbers below, the four-decimal one gives K2 `0.01670 + j0.13790` and solid bonding `0.04293 + j0.12837` (which is also K1, the sheaths at zero volts per minor section). Both K2 values round to the book's `0.017 + j0.138`.
- R-6 Kersting Example 4.2 (pp. 96 to 98, third edition: the conductor data on p. 96, the positions on p. 97, the result on p. 98), one pole, 7 conductors in this order: line 1 phases a, b, c at `0 + j35`, `2.5 + j35`, `7 + j35` ft (336,400 26/7 ACSR: GMR 0.0244 ft, 0.306 ohm/mile, diameter 0.721 in), line 2 phases a', b', c' at `2.5 + j33`, `7 + j33`, `0 + j33` ft as printed (the "rolled" arrangement, 250,000 AA: GMR 0.0171 ft, 0.41 ohm/mile, diameter 0.567 in), neutral at `4 + j29` ft (4/0 6/1 ACSR: GMR 0.00814 ft, 0.592 ohm/mile, diameter 0.563 in). "Phased alike" puts a', b', c' at `0 + j33`, `2.5 + j33`, `7 + j33` ft. "Line 2 on two phases" drops c' (6 conductors). R-1 gives the 7 x 7 `Z`, the neutral on earth at both ends is Kron-reduced.
- R-7 Tleis Example 3.4, the printed 7 x 7 of p. 139 (ohm/km, conductors 1 to 6 the phase bundles, 7 the earth wire): every self term of 1 to 6 is `0.0664 + j0.480`, `Z77 = 0.1136 + j0.6988`, every mutual has the resistance `0.0493` and the reactance: `x12 0.2286`, `x13 0.1928`, `x14 0.2138`, `x15 0.1906`, `x16 0.1777`, `x17 0.2147`, `x23 0.2407`, `x24 0.1906`, `x25 0.1898`, `x26 0.1892`, `x27 0.1780`, `x34 0.1777`, `x35 0.1892`, `x36 0.2023`, `x37 0.1609`, `x45 0.2286`, `x46 0.1928`, `x47 0.2147`, `x56 0.2407`, `x57 0.1780`, `x67 0.1609`. The earth wire is on earth at both ends and Kron-reduced. RYB/BYR puts B, Y, R on conductors 4, 5, 6.
- R-8 Transformers in the nodal solve of R-3: a one-phase two-winding unit between primary nodes `(p+, p-)` and secondary nodes `(s+, s-)` with ratio `n = V_leg,1 / V_leg,2` and series impedance `z = ((%R1 + %R2)/100 + j XHL/100) · kV2² / MVA` referred to the secondary stamps `y u uᵀ` with `y = 1/z` and `u = (1/n, -1/n, -1, 1)` on `(p+, p-, s+, s-)` (no magnetising branch). A set of nodes with no path to earth gets `1e-12` S to earth on each node to fix its common voltage, which moves no power. A three-phase bank is its three units. A tap `t` on a unit's secondary makes its ratio `n/t`. The joint projection of a bank between one pair of ports (section 1.13) imposes `I1 v` at the far port, solves the units' relations with the current law at the far port's nodes, and reads `Z_set = (1/3) vᴴ Δ / I1`.

Shared decks. Every step brief holds section 1, so a pin that names one of these decks reads it here. Each starts on its source as written and holds nothing else unless the pin adds it:
- `P-4W`: `STIFF("p4w")`, `new line.l4w phases=4 bus1=b.1.2.3.0 bus2=c.1.2.3.0 length=5 units=mi rmatrix=[0.4013 | 0.0953 0.4013 | 0.0953 0.0953 0.4013 | 0.0953 0.0953 0.0953 0.6873] xmatrix=[1.4133 | 0.8515 1.4133 | 0.7266 0.7802 1.4133 | 0.7524 0.7865 0.7674 1.5465] cmatrix=[15.0430 | -4.8706 15.8553 | -1.8657 -3.1007 14.3000 | -2.2414 -2.8501 -2.8662 14.1679]` (the printed primitive matrix of Kersting p. 93 and the nodal `C` of Kersting 5.1), the line alone. A pin writes its own bus variant (`bus1=b.1.2.3.4 bus2=c.1.2.3.4` for the private neutral, `bus2=c.1.2.3.4` for the neutral earthed at `b` only) and its own loads or fault. `load.ld` is `new load.ld bus1=c phases=3 kv=12.47 kw=3000 kvar=1500 model=2`, present only in a pin that names it.
- `lateral`: `STIFF("lat")`, `new line.lat phases=3 bus1=b.1.3.0 bus2=c.1.3.0 length=5 units=mi normamps=400 emergamps=600 rmatrix=[0.4013 | 0.0953 0.4013 | 0.0953 0.0953 0.6873] xmatrix=[1.4133 | 0.7266 1.4133 | 0.7524 0.7674 1.5465] cmatrix=[15.0430 | -1.8657 14.3000 | -2.2414 -2.8662 14.1679]` (the a, c, n rows and columns of P-4W), with the loads its pin names.
- `r1/rg`: `STIFF("r1rg")`, `new reactor.r1 phases=1 bus1=b.1 bus2=b.4 r=400 x=200`, `new reactor.rg phases=1 bus1=b.4 bus2=b.0 r=25 x=0.001`.
- `cs/rn`: `STIFF("csrn")`, `new capacitor.cs phases=3 bus1=b bus2=b.4.4.4 kvar=600 kv=12.47`, `new reactor.rn phases=1 bus1=b.4 bus2=b.0 r=0 x=5`.
- `dy`: `STIFF("dy")`, `new transformer.dy phases=3 windings=2 buses=[b x] conns=[delta wye] kvs=[12.47 4.16] kvas=[1000 1000] xhl=6`.
- `K42` (the Kersting 4.2 pole): `STIFF_AT(name, 24.94, 60)`, `new line.dc phases=7 bus1=b.1.2.3.1.2.3.0 bus2=c.1.2.3.4.5.6.0 length=10 units=mi` with the 7 x 7 `rmatrix` and `xmatrix` the test builds by R-1 from the R-6 data in ohm per mile (rolled as printed, or phased alike where the pin says so) and a `cmatrix` of zeros unless the pin builds it by R-2.
- `T35` (the Tleis 3.5 cable): `STIFF_AT(name, 12.47, 50)`, three Lines of 1 km with `units=km`, each the 6 x 6 of R-5 in ohm per km and its `cmatrix` in nF per km: `new line.s1 phases=6 bus1=b.1.2.3.0.0.0 bus2=j1.1.2.3.4.5.6`, `new line.s2 phases=6 bus1=j1.2.3.1.4.5.6 bus2=j2.2.3.1.4.5.6`, `new line.s3 phases=6 bus1=j2.3.1.2.4.5.6 bus2=e.3.1.2.0.0.0` (cores rotated, sheaths straight), the load on `e` its pin names.

Not carried as pins: Dommel Table 4.2 (500 kV bundle), the 400 kV cable of Ametani et al., 2nd ed. (2016), section 3.2.4.2.4, Clarke Vol. I Problem 7 and the open-conductor network of Clarke Vol. I eq. [47]. They confirm the three-phase instance, the gap of the two forms and the two-phase instance that Kersting 4.1, Tleis 3.4 and 3.5 and the lateral already pin with their data in this plan. Their input data are not reproduced here, so they stay literature references of section 1.15 and no step pins them.

## 2. Decisions

The user's word of 2026-10-04 leaves decisions D1 to D7 to the planner, and decision D6 is user-confirmed. The user's rule of 2026-10-10 makes the open technical questions of a plan the coordinator's to decide: under it the coordinator took on 2026-10-10 the plan's proposals for the four questions of section 8 (decisions D5 and D7, the confirmation of decision D21 and the order check of decision D11), and decisions D8 to D22, the questions section 1 leaves open, are decided under the same rule. Each step builds the decision as written here.

**D1. Chains of cable screens: K2.** Candidates: K2, the joint elimination with each Line taking its section's share (section 1.6), KC, the screens kept as a second circuit of each Line, K1, the screens at zero volts in each section. Physics: the sheath current of a cross-bonded major section is set by the whole chain (the sum of the three sections' induced voltages), so no per-section rule can see it. Numbers: Tleis Example 3.5 K2 `0.01670 + j0.13833` ohm/km against the book's `0.017 + j0.138`, K1 `0.04299 + j0.12878` (2.57 times the resistance, the cable loss 2.57 times too high on the three-section deck, `dS/S` 1.8e-3 against 3.3e-7 for K2), CIGRE TB 531 Table 10 gives K2's value for cross-bonding, KC appears in no book as a positive-sequence model and depends on the conductor order of the screens. Decision: K2, with the tap inside a chain as stated limit L8.

**D2. A one- or two-phase Fault stops the command.** Candidates: A, stop (section 1.12 case 8), B, the projection `3R` to earth or `R` between phases (one rule for every class, its shunt impedance off by `(Z2 + Z0) / (3R)` for a large `R` and by more than the whole fault current for the default `r=0.0001` ohm, whose `3R` is far below any network's `Z2 + Z0`), C, the network shunt `3R + Z2 + Z0` (exact for a symmetric network, but it reads the whole network and goes stale after any edit). Physics: a fault is unbalanced by meaning, and every book keeps the other sequence networks for it (IEEE Std 551 p. 61). Decision: A.

**D3. A refusal stops the command and changes nothing.** Candidates: (i) stop and change nothing (section 1.12), (ii) refuse the element and leave it multi-phase (the step-3 behaviour for refused matrices), (iii) disable it. Physics: (ii) puts a multi-phase element on nodes that no longer exist and turns a one-phase Fault of `R` into a three-phase fault of `R`, and (iii) removes power. Decision: (i), all or nothing (section 1.12).

**D4. A delta Capacitor `cmatrix` is nodal for N >= 3 and refused for N = 1, 2.** The documentation reads "Cmatrix Alternate method of defining a capacitor bank. Enter nodal capacitance matrix in uf. Can be used to define either series or shunt banks." A nodal matrix has one meaning whatever `conn` says. `[10|-2 10|-2 -2 10]` gives `C1 = S(C) = 12` µF and `ω C1 V_LL² = 703.47` kvar at 12.47 kV. A leg reading differs by the factor 3 and is not what the documentation says. A nodal matrix of order N has no place for the N + 1 conductors of a one- or two-phase delta bank, so those stay refused. Decision: nodal for N >= 3, refused for N = 1, 2 (MPS-07).

**D5. The non-reciprocal inter-circuit term: the symmetric part, no refusal (section 8 Q2).** Candidates: write the symmetric part (the books' practice, Clarke eq. [98], Tleis's transposition, the user's word of 2026-10-04), or refuse when `ρ = |A12| / |S12|` exceeds a share. The plan builds the symmetric part and pins the sizes as stated limit L1: Tleis Example 3.4 `ρ = 0.158`, Kersting Example 4.2 `ρ = 1.732` with 4.7e-3 and 8.05e-3 of the source power lost in reactive power under constant-power loads. Against two uncoupled Lines the symmetric part is better by source power in one of the four constant-power cases of section 1.16 (equal power factors, 4.7e-5 against 3.8e-3) and worse in three (4.7e-3 against 1.8e-3, 8.05e-3 against 7.3e-3, 1.8e-3 against 6.1e-4), while by load voltage it is closer on circuit 1 in every case (-65 against -104 V, -64 against -101 V, -84 against -109 V, -110 against -161 V), and on circuit 2 closer in one case (+74 against +103 V), equal within 2 V in two (+92 against +90 V, +23 against +23 V) and farther in one (+83 against +58 V). The lost power depends on the load currents, which the command does not know, so no share bounds it. `ρ` also measures the antisymmetric part against a symmetric part that can be near zero, not the error: a three-phase and a two-phase circuit on the Kersting 4.2 pole have `ρ = 6.67` with only 5.0e-4 of the source power off, so any share that refuses Kersting 4.2 (`ρ = 1.732`) refuses that ordinary pair too. Decision (section 8 Q2): the symmetric part is always written and no share refuses an element.

**D6. The rating of merged cores (user-confirmed).** `rating = min_k (rating_k / share_k)` times `N_c/3`. The engine holds one rating per Line, so `rating_k` is the Line's for every core and the bundle rating is `rating / max_k share_k`. Nine flat cores of one wire: 2.7101 core ratings against the plain sum 3. Kersting Example 6.9: `rating / 0.5839` with one rating. Its two wires of 530 A and 329 A would give 695.17 A against 859 A, a case the engine cannot hold and no pin builds. MPS-04 writes the formula and its pins.

**D7. A two-phase Line whose loads all sit between its phases: the 120-degree form (section 8 Q1).** Candidates: the 120-degree form `(3/4)(Z11 + Z22 - Z12)` of section 1.8 (its instance of two phases 120 degrees apart) with the stated limit L3, or the loop form `Z11 + Z22 - 2 Z12` when every element downstream of the Line lies between its two phases. For uncoupled conductors the loop form equals the rule of IEEE Std 551 section 3.7, p. 68 ("use the impedance of one conductor (one phase to neutral, NOT the round loop impedance)", the three-phase result times `√3/2`) in this reduction's bookkeeping, and the mutual term is own derivation. On the lateral of section 1.16: one load between the phases, 120-degree form `dS/S = 2.3e-3`, loop form `3.0e-5`. Two loads to earth, 120-degree form `3.4e-4`. The loop form needs a scan of everything downstream and is wrong as soon as one load to earth is added. Decision (section 8 Q1): the static 120-degree form `(3/4)(Z11 + Z22 - Z12)` for every two-phase circuit, with L3 pinned.

**D8. The repeat guard is the circuit model.** On a circuit already reduced the command changes nothing, so a reduced `phases=2` coupled element is never read as a two-phase lateral. Candidates: a new flag set by the command (lost by `Save circuit` and a reload, after which a second command would multiply every one-phase impedance by 3 again), a test of the elements (impossible: a genuine one-phase circuit looks reduced), or `Circuit::positive_sequence`, the `CktModel` the documentation defines ("Designates whether circuit model is to interpreted as a normal multi-phase model or a positive-sequence only model") and that `Save circuit` writes as `Set Cktmodel=Positive`. Decision: the guard reads `positive_sequence`. A circuit the user declared positive-sequence by `Set CktModel=Positive` is reduced by declaration, so the command changes nothing there either. The command sets the flag only after its analysis succeeded.

**D9. Disabled elements and open conductors belong to the circuit as built.** The meaning of a node is the wiring (which phase conductor it is), not the switching state, which changes during a study. Decision: a disabled element and an open conductor carry phasors in the map, are reduced, and are refused like enabled ones. A reduced element keeps its enabled state and the open state of its conductors circuit by circuit (section 1.11). A circuit open on some of its phases and closed on others at one terminal has no balanced pattern and stops the command (section 1.12). An island that no source and no power conversion element reaches has no phasor, so its elements end on node 0 and are disabled (decision D13).

**D10. The remaining classes.** Section 1.12 forbids leaving a multi-phase element on a reduced circuit, and section 1.1 gives every class the same projection. Decision, class by class (MPS-03, MPS-06 and MPS-08 build them, each with a pin on a stiff bus):
- IndMach012: reduced to `phases=1`, `kv` by the leg rule, `kw` and `kva` divided by 3, its per-unit impedances, `H`, `D` and slip kept. An induction machine under a balanced voltage is its positive-sequence model, which is the one-phase form, so the reduced machine draws a third of the balanced power (T3), and the engine's one-phase branch (it computes from the phase voltage where the three-phase machine reads `V1`) is held to that by the pin.
- VCCS: `phases=1`, `prated` divided by 3, `vrated` by the leg rule, `ppct`, the curves and `imaxpu` kept (exact, T3).
- UPFC: a series device with `Through` legs, `phases=1`, `kvarlimit` divided by 3, and `refkv`, `refkv2`, `vpqmax`, `xs` and `climit` kept. The engine compares `refkv` with the magnitude of each conductor's voltage to earth (`elements::pc::upfc`, `curr_out_for_ref`: the error `refkv · 1000 - |Vbin|` clamped to `±vpqmax`), so `refkv`, `refkv2` and `vpqmax` are phase quantities, and the reduced port voltage is that phase voltage. `climit` is a phase current and `xs` an impedance in series with each conductor, which the impedance form keeps for a three-phase device (exact on a stiff bus, T3).
- VSConverter: `phases=2` with `ndc=1` (one AC and one DC conductor, as today), `kw`, `pacref`, `qacref` and `idcmax` divided by 3, `kvac` by the leg rule, `rac`, `xac`, `iacmax`, `kvdc` and the modulation data kept. Its AC conductors are legs to earth (`minus` none), the DC conductor no leg.
- GICLine, GICsource: `phases=1`, the EMF projected (zero sequence, so `volts=0`, section 1.9), the series impedance in the impedance form.
- GICTransformer: `phases=1`, its winding resistances times the projection factor `k` (1 for three phases), section 1.13.
- Vsource, Isource: section 1.9. An EMF or injection of zero or negative sequence projects to zero.
A class whose one-phase form in the engine fails its pin is not refused silently: the step reports it to the coordinator as an engine defect of that one-phase form, and refuses the class with the D10 message of section 1.12 only until the defect is fixed. Controls and meters follow their reduced target (decision D16) and read through it the quantity they read before (decision D22).

**D11. Which terminals bind a port, which conductors are lumped, the order of the phases.** Section 1.4 names "one multi-phase terminal" and section 1.3 names five classes. Decision: a terminal of a source, a transformer winding or a power conversion element binds its phase nodes into one port, since such a terminal is one three-phase device. The conductors of a passive PD element (Line, Reactor, Capacitor, Fault, GICTransformer) follow their conductor order three by three, so a six-conductor reactor holds two circuits like a six-conductor Line. Every power conversion class counts as a lumped carrier in section 1.3 (a leg of a PVSystem is a two-terminal conductor as a leg of a Load is). A check of every multi-phase terminal for one port in positive order has no positive order to test two phases against. A two-phase element whose stamp carries no rotation draws the same balanced power on `(1, a)` as on `(1, a²)`, `Y1 = (1/3) Σ_k |λ_k|² y`, so a two-phase Load on `c.1.3` (the corpus holds two, `asymmetric/load/midi_load_asym.dss` and `asymmetric/reactor/midi_reactor_asym.dss`) is accepted. Section 8 Q4 takes the same argument for three phases: the order is checked only for an element whose model depends on it, against the pattern that model imposes (section 1.4 item 3). A three-phase Load or a winding on `c.1.3.2` is reduced, and a Vsource, Isource, VSConverter, IndMach012, or a `Balanced=yes` PVSystem, Storage or `model=7` Generator there stops the command.

**D12. Port numbering and the bus spelling.** Ports of a bus take the numbers 1, 2, ... in the order of their lowest original node, so a bus whose phases sit on nodes 1, 2, 3 keeps node 1 and today's spelling. A terminal whose reduced nodes equal the element's default is written bare, any other explicitly (section 1.11). The documentation calls nodes above the phases "points of connection with no special meaning", so reusing node 2 for port 2 is within the language.

**D13. Seeds.** The Vsources seed in creation order, each only where the map has not reached its nodes. A Vsource whose nodes the map has all reached through a winding or a Line seeds nothing and is projected, so a second source behind a delta-wye bank takes its angle less the map's phasor `θ` of its node (section 1.9). A Vsource reached on some of its nodes seeds only the others, with its pattern turned to the phasor of its first carried node (section 1.2 step 1), and is then projected. After the sources, a multi-phase terminal of two or three phases of a power conversion element that nothing reached seeds its own island (a terminal above three phases is no balanced set and stops the command, case 3, section 1.4 item 1). An island reached by neither is internal and its elements are disabled, since no balanced state puts a voltage on it.

**D14. Power conversion elements on internal nodes.** Candidates: the element's nominal admittance in the elimination (exact for the balanced state), or the internal node read at zero volts. Physics: the nominal admittance is the element at its rated voltage, so it gives the star the voltage the balanced state puts there. It fits a nodal elimination (voltage imposed), not the chain's series solve (current imposed). Decision: a star private to one power conversion element (a wye Load on `b.1.2.3.4` with `b.4` touched by nothing else) is eliminated inside the element by its nominal admittance. A star that lumped and power conversion elements share, with no Line conductor on it, is eliminated jointly with their nominal admittances (section 1.6). Both are exact for constant impedance. An element of equal legs on its own star is written at the per-unit voltage its legs sit at (section 1.13) and is exact for every load model, and an element whose legs sit at different per-unit voltages (on a joint star with other elements) is stated limit L11 for another load model. A power conversion or lumped conductor between a port node and a chain node, a node that a non-phase Line conductor reaches, reads that node at zero volts and enters the chain solve as an open conductor: exact for a balanced set of equal legs on a line whose phases induce no voltage in its neutral (a symmetric geometry), off by the neutral's induced drop and by the unbalance otherwise (stated limit L9, on the P-4W line with three balanced loads 6.3e-5). A two-phase wye load of 600 kW on its own floating star draws 450 kW in the balanced state at constant impedance (each leg at `√3/2` of its rated voltage) and the zero-volt reading would give it 600 kW, which is why a nodal star takes the admittance.

**D15. A lumped conductor between ports of different phasors, and the scale of a joint star.** The projection of a one-phase branch `y` between port nodes of phasors `w_p`, `w_q` of two different ports is `(y/3)[1, -conj(w_p) w_q; -conj(w_q) w_p, 1]`. It is a branch of its class only when `w_p = w_q` (the impedance form then applies). For any other angle, 180 degrees included, it is a branch plus shunts or a non-reciprocal pair, which no element of the class holds. Decision: refused under the criterion (section 1.12). The same reasoning fixes the scale of a joint star (section 1.6 item 4): a power conversion carrier holds any complex share with an active part of its own sign, a `cuf` Capacitor only a positive multiple of `jω` and a Reactor only a branch of its own `R/X`, so the share of a disabled element goes to the power conversion carriers when there are any, by a positive real scale of all carriers otherwise, and the command stops when neither keeps each carrier in its class.

**D16. Controls and meters are applied after the elements.** Today the conversion runs in creation order, so a control created before its target sees the unconverted target. Decision: the command applies every PD and PC element first (in creation order), then every control and meter, so each control reads its target reduced. A control names an existing element when it is defined, so a deck reaches the old order only if the language lets a control be defined before its element or an element be created again after its control. MPS-01 checks this on r4133 with epri-worker: if a deck reaches it, the reduced control differs between the two orders and the step writes report 101 with that deck, else the record says the order is not reachable and number 101 stays unused.

**D17. The voltage and power of a reduced leg.** Candidates for the rated voltage of the reduced element: the voltage its reference leg would see with every star at zero volts (`V_leg,r / |λ_r|`), or the voltage its legs see in the balanced state (`V_leg,r / |Δw_r|`). Physics: the element's load model reads its terminal voltage per unit of its rating, so the reduced element must sit at the per-unit voltage its legs sit at. On a two-leg 600 kW load on its own star each leg sits at `√3/2`: the first candidate writes `kw 150` at `kv 7.19956` (rated power over 4, against the rated power over 3 of section 1.13) and misses a constant-power load by 2.5e-1, the second writes `kw 200` at `kv 8.31333` and is exact for constant impedance and constant power alike (section 1.16). Both give the same admittance. Decision: the leg rule of section 1.13, `kV' = V_leg,r / |Δw_r|` on the leg of largest per-unit voltage `|Δw_k| / V_leg,k`, and the written power `S' = conj(f) · (1/3) Σ_k (|Δw_k| kV'/V_leg,k)² S_leg,k`. It gives `kV/√3` for every three-phase element, `kV` for a one-phase element to earth, `kV/√3` for a one-phase element between two phases, `kV/2` between two poles, `2 kV/3` for two equal legs on their own star, and the turns ratio of a one-phase transformer between two phases times `√3`, which section 1.2 shows is the ratio the ideal winding imposes, each with the rated power over 3 and exact for every load model since each leg keeps its per-unit voltage. With legs at different per-unit voltages (a two-phase delta element, an element on a joint star with others) it is the nominal admittance's power at the largest leg's voltage, exact for constant impedance (stated limit L11). For those legs a third candidate keeps both the admittance and the rated power over 3: `kV'` at the power-weighted RMS per-unit voltage of the legs, `u' = (Σ_k u_k² |S_leg,k| / Σ_k |S_leg,k|)^(1/2)` with `u_k = |Δw_k| V_port / V_leg,k`, and `S' = conj(f) (1/3) Σ_k S_leg,k`. On the two-phase delta Load of L11 (legs at 1.0 and 0.57735 pu) it writes `kv 8.81762`, `kw 200` where the leg rule writes `kv 7.19956`, `kw 133.333`. Neither dominates at constant power: the leg rule is exact for every `vminpu` above the lowest leg (the default 0.95 included), the RMS rule for a `vminpu` at or below the lowest leg or above the RMS voltage (0.57735 and 0.81650 on that deck), and the largest error is 1/3 for the leg rule (a `vminpu` at or below 0.57735: 400 kW reduced against 600 unreduced) and 1/2 for the RMS rule (a `vminpu` between 0.57735 and 0.81650: 600 kW reduced against 400 unreduced). At constant current neither is exact (473.205 kW unreduced, 400.000 by the leg rule, 489.898 by the RMS rule), and at constant impedance and at the default `vminpu` both are. The plan keeps the leg rule: a leg rated above the voltage the circuit puts across it, as the leg to earth of a two-phase delta element, sits in the constant-impedance band at the default and at every `vminpu` above its own per-unit voltage, the leg rule follows that split exactly, and its largest error is the smaller one. The cost is that the rated power over 3 of section 1.13 holds only where the legs sit at one per-unit voltage (section 1.14), which L11 states and pins. An AutoTrans reads its terminals, since its series winding has `Δw = 0`.

**D18. Switch Lines have no rule of their own.** A Line with `switch=yes` stamps the matrices its switch constants give, and the kept-value rule of section 1.8 writes them: the same `r1`, `x1`, `c1` and length as today when the switch was not edited after `switch=yes`. Today the reduction writes `r1=1 x1=1 c1=1.1 length=0.001` over a switch whose constants were edited after `switch=yes`, a fault both oracles share. A switch whose constants were edited keeps its own values (MPS-03, report 103).

**D19. The open questions (1) to (9) of the step-3 row, the phase-to-phase reactor reading and the reduced Vsource data.** Item (7) holds the coordinator's decision of 2026-10-10 under the user's rule of that date (the head of section 2), and this plan decides the others. The choices the row marks taken stand until the step named here lands its answer, and that step rewrites the item in the row.
- (1) One- and two-phase totals: the projection keeps the balanced power of every phase count (sections 1.8 and 1.13), so a two-phase `cuf` bank, a two-phase `gmatrix` fault (refused, section 1.12 case 8) and a two-phase Line are no longer reduced per phase, and the two-phase delta `kvar` Reactor of the row (746.3 + j7618.9 kVA before the command) keeps its power by the leg rule, where today it draws 2036.8 + j11165.7 kVA after (MPS-03).
- (2) Taken by section 1.14 (the delta Reactor matrix of legs) and decision D4 (the nodal delta `cmatrix`), MPS-07.
- (3) A Reactor above three conductors: its conductors fall into circuits three by three (D11) and each takes the form its map gives (section 1.8): a shunt Reactor the admittance form on its nodal matrix with `W` from the map, a series one the impedance form. Neither reading of the row (each matrix reduced alone, 0.825 + j8.25, or the admittance over all four conductors, 0.7939 + j7.9391, for its unbalanced 4 x 4) is a rule of its own, the map decides what the fourth conductor is (MPS-03).
- (4) A missing `rmatrix` or `xmatrix`: the other part is a zero matrix, no message (MPS-07 item 5). The parallel-form inversion pivots on the diagonal and calls an invertible matrix with a zero diagonal entry singular. Such a matrix has a negative eigenvalue (`[0|1 0]` has -1), so it is not passive and its refusal stands. MPS-07 checks passivity first and gives it the passivity message `Reactor.p: rmatrix is not passive, so the parallel reactor draws power it cannot hold. Specify a passive rmatrix. Aborting solution.`
- (5) Taken by section 1.12 case 6 (MPS-01), and Dump, `?` and Save print such a matrix at its stored order (section 1.14).
- (6) `rp`, `RCurve` and `LCurve` on a matrix Reactor are not in its stamp, so they are not in its reduction. The reduced Reactor of the series form writes `rp=0` for no parallel branch and no curves, as step 3 does (section 1.1: the reduction is the projection of what the element stamps), MPS-03 item 4.
- (7) is decided: the I²R label of a `Parallel=yes` matrix Reactor stays as built, since its branch carries the terminal current and its loss is I²R by meaning. The reduction writes its parallel resistance as `rp` (MPS-03 item 4), whose loss the engine reads as no-load.
- (8) is decision D21 (MPS-03).
- (9) a two-phase `z1` Reactor stamps the diagonal `(Z0 + Z1 + Z2)/3` per phase, uncoupled. The reduction projects that stamp: two phases 120 degrees apart to earth give `Y1 = 2 y_d / 3`, so `z' = 1.5 z_d`. Whether the stamp should hold the 2 x 2 block of the three-phase sequence matrix is a question of the language (the documentation has no two-phase rule) outside this plan, and the reduction follows whatever the stamp is (MPS-03, `a_two_phase_z1_reactor_reduces_its_diagonal_stamp`).
- A Reactor from `b.1` to `b.2` reduces to `b.1` to earth with `y` (`(1/3)|1 - a²|² y`), and its loss reads three times the reduced loss, the unreduced loss, where both oracles read 0 with a negative I²R (MPS-03, `a_phase_to_phase_reactor_reads_its_loss_after_the_reduction`). The first step that touches the step-3 DIVERGENCES row (MPS-01) writes "reads the loss of" in its Exclusion paragraph (MPS-01 item 9).
- A reduced Vsource writes `phases=1`, `basekv`, `angle`, `r1` and `x1`. Its zero-sequence data stay as given, since a one-phase source stamps `Z1` alone.

**D20. Limits outside L1 to L6 of section 6 are stated and pinned, not refused.** L7 (the form rule is a static proxy), L8 (a tap inside a chain), L9 (a power conversion or lumped element at a chain node read at zero volts), L10 (one rating for a coupled element), L11 (legs at different per-unit voltages, read by the nominal admittance), L12 (an open-delta bank, an element whose own stamp is not balanced), L13 (a control on a coupled element holds one setting for circuits whose readings scale differently). Each is the cost of a static rule that reads no solved state, of an element that holds one value, or of an element with no positive-sequence form beyond its projection (as the unbalanced loads of L4), each is measured on its deck, and a refusal would need a threshold that section 1 does not give. L12 would refuse the open-delta regulator banks of ordinary feeders (IEEE 37 holds one, with the jumper of its common phase that the joint projection of section 1.13 disables).

**D21. The four-conductor line with its neutral retained (open question 8 of step 3).** Section 1.5 decides it by physics: a neutral on node 0 at both ends is earthed at both ends, Kron-reduced in `Z` and struck in `C`. On the Kersting Example 4.1 line of 5 miles at 12.47 kV with 3000 + j1500 kVA of constant-impedance load, the reduction keeps the source power to `6.4e-5` and the mean over four conductors misses it by `8.3e-3` with the line loss 21 per cent high (section 1.16). The step-3 measurement on its own deck (2683.6 + j1341.8 kVA and 99.0 kW unreduced, 2631.9 + j1316.0 kVA and 119.7 kW by the mean over four) agrees in kind. A plain matrix Line above three conductors marks no neutral, and under sections 1.2 to 1.5 the meaning of its fourth conductor comes from its nodes, not from the matrix. Books: Kersting eq. (4.55), p. 86, Dommel eq. (4.28b), p. 4-19, and Clarke Vol. I p. 421 (a multigrounded neutral "can be treated as a ground wire") with eq. [100], [101], p. 426 (a one-phase tap enters the sequence networks as `3 Za0` on the Kron-reduced conductor). MPS-03 builds it. The coordinator confirmed it on 2026-10-10 (section 8 Q3).

**D22. Controls read through the reduced target the quantity they read before, and dispatch power at the balanced total.** Candidates: keep every control setting as given (the step base and both oracles), or rescale each setting that a control compares with a quantity it reads through its reduced target. Physics: a setting is a physical quantity (a voltage behind a PT, a current behind a CT, a power), and the reduction changes what the control reads by a factor the map fixes. The reduced winding of a regulator between two phases stands from its port to earth, so its PT reads `1/|Δw|` of the winding voltage: on IEEE 37 (`reg1a`, `reg1c`, 4.8 kV delta windings, `ptratio=40`, `vreg=122`) the PT reads 69.3 V at nominal, the regulator runs to its tap limit and the regulated bus sits about 10 per cent high where the unreduced regulator holds 122/120 of nominal. A circuit of `N_c < 3` phases carries `i' = (N_c/3) i`, so a recloser on a two-phase Line trips at 1.5 times its pickup current. A power is different: `CktElement::terminal_power` multiplies the reduced terminal's power by 3 on a positive-sequence circuit (section 1.0), so every power a control reads through its target is already the balanced total its setting is written in. Decision: rescale what the control reads as a voltage or a current, keep what it reads as a power, by these factors (own derivation from section 1.1 and the leg rule):
- `α_V`, a voltage the control reads: 1 for a conductor of a port read to earth (the port voltage is the phase voltage), `1/|w_p - w_q|` for a voltage read between two phase conductors of one port, `1/|Δw_r|` for a winding read across its leg.
- `α_I`, a current: `N_c/3` for a conductor of a circuit of `N_c` phases. For a winding it is the reduced winding's terminal current over its real terminal current, since a control reads terminal currents and never leg currents (the RegControl divides the controlled conductor of its transformer's `terminal_currents` by `ctprim`, `elements::control::reg_control::control_loop`, and a CapControl, Relay or Recloser on a transformer terminal reads the same buffer): 1 for a three-phase wye or delta winding (both carry the line current, and so does the reduced winding at `kVA/3` and `kV/√3`), `2/3` for a two-phase wye winding, `|Δw_r| / 3` for a one-phase winding alone between its ports, whose terminal current is its leg current (`1/3` to earth, `1/√3` between two phases, `2/3` across two poles). In general `(n |Δw_r| / 3) · |I_leg / I_terminal|` for a winding of `n` legs that carries its circuit's current alone: its reduced current carries `(1/3) Σ_k S_leg,k` at `V_leg,r/|Δw_r|`, which is `n |Δw_r| / 3` of the leg current, and the terminal current of a delta winding is `√3` times its leg current by the current law at a delta corner (as Kersting eq. (8.104), p. 281, writes it for the open delta, and Clarke Vol. I p. 451: a symmetrical Y of three times the admittance of the Δ), so the delta winding of a Dy bank keeps its `ctprim`, where the leg-current factor `√3` would write it `√3` too large and the compensator would read `1/√3` of the line current. For a unit of a bank reduced in parallel between one pair of ports it is its reduced current over its real current in the set's solve (section 1.13), `1/2` for an equal open-delta pair: on IEEE 37 (`creg1a`, `creg1c`, `ctprim=350`) the CT of each unit sees half the real unit current, so `ctprim` becomes 175 and the compensator `R`, `X` in volts stay.
- `α_S = 1`, a power: the reading is the balanced total. A power reader that does not give the balanced total on a positive-sequence circuit is a defect of that reader, and the step fixes it. One exists: the StorageController reads a one-phase monitored element through `terminal_power` and then multiplies by 3 again (`solution::controls::dispatch::StorageDispEnv::control_power`), so on a reduced circuit it reads nine times the reduced power, three times the balanced total. r4133 does the same (its `GetControlPower` takes `Power[ElementTerminal]` for one phase and multiplies by 3 under `PositiveSequence`), an upstream bug fixed by MPS-10 and reported in report 110.
- a phase selector (CapControl `PTPhase`, `CTPhase`, RegControl `PTphase`) that names a conductor above the reduced target's conductor count reads conductor 1: the balanced state puts one magnitude on every phase, so the selected phase reads the one reduced conductor. `AVG`, `MAX` and `MIN` stay, and a line-to-line reading of a delta bank is carried by `α_V` on the PT ratio. A selector left above the count indexes a buffer sized to the reduced target (the step base: `CapControl::get_control_voltage` with `PTPhase=2` on a one-phase target reads a slot never written, 0 V, and switches the bank in, `PTPhase=3` or `CTPhase=3` panics, `RegControl::get_control_voltage` with `PTphase=2` or 3 panics), and the corpus holds such controls (`ieee9500dss/ieee9500_base.dss`, `epri_dpv/J1/Capacitors.dss`).
- a balanced-total reading compared with the controlled or monitored element's own rated or present power converts by `k` (3 on a positive-sequence circuit, 1 otherwise) as the dispatch below does. One exists: the CapControl `Pf` type closes the bank only when the reactive flow exceeds `k · TotalKvar · PctMinkvar / 100` and, closed, only when it exceeds `k · TotalKvar / NumSteps · 0.5`, since `TotalKvar` is the reduced bank's own kvar (a third of the bank) while the flow is the balanced total. A 600 kvar bank with the default 50 per cent closes at 300 kvar of line reactive power unreduced, and the step base closes it at 100 kvar on the reduced circuit. r4133 compares the same two quantities (an upstream bug fixed by MPS-10, report 110).
A ratio the reading is divided by (`PTRatio`, `CTRatio`, `CTPrim`, `RemotePTRatio`) and a setting in primary units compared with a voltage or a current (volts, amps) are multiplied by the factor of what they meet, and a setting in ohm compared with a voltage over a current by `α_V/α_I`. A power setting (kW, kvar, kVA, a seasonal kW target) stays. Settings after the PT and CT (a regulator's `vreg`, `band` and compensator `R`, `X` on its 120 V base), per-unit settings on the target's own base (the leg rule keeps each leg's per-unit voltage), times, counts, curves and multipliers of a curve stay. A residual (zero-sequence) or negative-sequence quantity is zero in the balanced state the reduced circuit stands for, so a control on a positive-sequence circuit reads it as zero: a ground pickup and a 46 or 47 element never act there, where the step base reads the one reduced conductor's current as the residual. A control whose target holds circuits of different factors (a coupled element) takes the factor of its first circuit (stated limit L13).

Dispatch. A dispatcher reads its deficit in balanced-total kW and adds it to the elements it commands, whose own powers stay per reduced phase (section 1.0). The GenDispatcher adds its deficit to each Generator's `kw_base` and `kvar_base`, and the StorageController adds its power deficit to each Storage's `present_kw` and the fleet's own kW to its power deficit. On a reduced circuit each step therefore moves three times the power it reads: on the deck of `a_gen_dispatcher_settles_its_generator_at_the_balanced_total` (a deficit of 500 kW) the reduced generator jumps between 1 and 1498 kW per phase and the solve ends at its control-iteration limit, where the unreduced deck settles in one step. r4133 does the same (its `TGenDispatcherObj.Sample` adds `PDiff` to `kWBase`), an upstream bug reported in report 110. Decision: with `k = 3` on a positive-sequence circuit and 1 otherwise, a dispatcher's power deficit enters an element as `deficit / k`, and the fleet's own kW enters a power deficit as `k` times its sum. The GenDispatcher's floors of 1 kW and 0 kvar are balanced totals (`1/k` and 0 of the element's own). The StorageController's current modes stay, since their kW come from a conductor current times the phase voltage of the reduced port, the element's own unit on both sides. The ESPVLControl writes a kW base that moves nothing on the circuit, and stays. MPS-10 builds it, report 110.

## 3. Per-step ritual and tiers

Each step is one `step.json` for the local script builder `.inputs/workflows/mk_step.py` (gitignored tooling), built into a workflow script and launched by hand after a resource check (CLAUDE.md "Launching agents and workflows"). The script's stages and their models:

| stage | role | model / effort |
|---|---|---|
| Plan | the step's planner writes `plan.md` with sections (1) to (8) and parts P1..Pn of about 250k tokens | opus / xhigh |
| Plan review | findings only | opus / max |
| Plan revise | corrects the plan | opus / xhigh |
| Plan check | a fresh agent, a major stops the run | opus / xhigh |
| Exec | one executor per part (at most eight), one commit per part, a continuation when a part runs out | opus / xhigh |
| Gate | the full seven commands of CLAUDE.md "## Gate" through `gatelock`, `DSS_ORACLE_TIMEOUT_SECS=600`, a gate fix and a second gate | opus / medium (gate), opus / xhigh (fix) |
| Audit | code audit and test audit, split by diff size (above 1500 changed lines `ceil(diff/2000)` agents per role) | opus / max |
| Settle | settlement in parts of six findings, gate | opus / max |
| Settlement audit | fresh agent | opus / max |
| Settle 2 | second round | opus / max |
| Landing | by hand: a fresh audit of the last settlement, one squash commit whose subject names the code change, the lane gate reused when the tree equals the gated head and `update` has not moved, else the full gate, push | coordinator, opus / high |

The plan's own requirements on every step:
- The full gate at every step (the scripts know no gate kind). `pwsh -File tools/lanes/lane_diff.ps1` runs at the steps that change a compat kernel or the solver, or add a caller of one: none of these steps does. The new linear algebra of the plan (the map's normal equations, the chain solve, the merge, every inversion of a projection) goes through `elements::pos_seq::project::lu_solve` and `inverse`, which MPS-01 adds outside the lane seam with partial pivoting, the same arithmetic in both lanes. No new code calls `CMatrix::invert` or `CMatrix::kron` (whose Gauss-Jordan kernel the lanes split), and the code audits of MPS-02 to MPS-05 check that by `rg -n "\.invert\(|\.kron\(" crates/dss-core/src/exec/make_pos_seq crates/dss-core/src/elements/pos_seq`, which finds nothing. The kept three-phase arithmetic of section 1.8 runs on the paths it runs on at the step base. So lane_diff does not run unless a step's planner finds a lane alias moved.
- At most eight parts per step (the step script cuts at eight). A step section lists its parts within that ceiling.
- Every cargo command the coordinator runs by hand (a landing gate, a lock regeneration, a register re-derivation) goes through the queue the step scripts hold, `python tmp/retro_fix/bin/gatelock.py --label "<tag> <role>" -- cargo <args>`, until one copy of the queue serves both.
- The record goes to `docs/phase-records/retro-fixes.md` and one bullet to the retro-fixes entry of `STATUS.md`, as the scripts' rules fix it. The record names the step by its title, not by `MPS-nn`.
- The upstream reports the step's section names, with the numbers reserved for it (section 0).
- Script extensions the plan needs (a coordinator change to the local tooling before MPS-01 launches, neither changes a stage): (a) `base` read at launch from `git rev-parse update` when `step.json` gives `"base": "update"`, (b) the `task` field may point at a file: the coordinator copies sections 0 to 3, the conventions at the head of section 4, the step's own section, section 5, the rows of sections 6 and 7 that name the step (with the header of each table and the paragraphs under it) and sections 8 and 9 of this plan into `tmp/retro_fix/state/<state>/step_brief.md`, and the `task` reads `Your step is in tmp/retro_fix/state/<state>/step_brief.md. Read it first and in full.` Section 1 holds the procedure every step builds on and is copied whole, so a brief measures 154 to 184 kB (MPS-09 the smallest, MPS-03 the largest), about 45 to 50 thousand tokens of an agent's budget of 200 to 300 thousand.

Launch checklist, by the coordinator, for each step:
1. The resource check of CLAUDE.md, and the steps the step's "after" line names have landed on `update`.
2. The lane worktree: `git worktree add .claude/worktrees/<lane> -b <lane> update`, then the directory junctions `.inputs`, `.venv` and `tools/opendss/.venv` to main's copies, as the earlier lanes hold them (the scripts' ritual step 0 checks `.inputs` inside the worktree).
3. The state folder `tmp/retro_fix/state/<state>/` with `step_brief.md` (extension (b)) and the `step.json` of the step's header, `task` set to the pointer sentence.
4. `python .inputs/workflows/mk_step.py <step.json> <out.js>` (the script reads the step file and the output path), then `Workflow({scriptPath: <out.js>})`.
5. After the landing, the worktree goes by CLAUDE.md "Git worktrees — safe deletion": the junctions dropped in PowerShell, `Test-Path` false on each, then `git worktree remove`, `git worktree prune`, and `git branch -D <lane>`.

## 4. Steps

Conventions shared by every step:
- Paths are under `crates/dss-core/src/` unless written otherwise. Items are named the rustdoc way. Two files are named `project.rs` and are always written with their folder: `exec/make_pos_seq/project.rs` (the projection stage of the command) and `elements/pos_seq/project.rs` (the arithmetic).
- `STIFF(name)` is `elements::pos_seq::balance::stiff(name)`: a 12.47 kV, 60 Hz source on bus `b` at 1e12 MVA. On it a shunt element sees one balanced voltage before and after the reduction, so a shunt reduction is exact (T3) and every one- or two-phase limit that needs source impedance vanishes. `STIFF_AT(name, kv, hz)` is `balance::stiff_at(name, kv, hz)` (added by MPS-01): the same circuit at `kv` line to line after `set defaultbasefrequency=hz`. `SOLVE` is `balance::solve_clean(dss, when)` on the 12.47 kV base, `SOLVE_AT(kv)` is `balance::solve_clean_at(dss, &[kv, ...], when)` (added by MPS-01) on the given voltage bases.
- `dS/S` is `balance::ds_over_s(deck: &str, reduce: &[&str], bases: &[f64]) -> f64` (added by MPS-01): it compiles `deck` in a fresh `Dss`, solves on `bases`, reads `S_full = balance::power(dss, "vsource.source")`, runs the commands of `reduce` in order, solves, reads `S_red` the same way (the engine reports the reduced circuit three times one phase) and returns `|S_red - S_full| / |S_full|`. Every command must leave no error.
- A twin is the reduced circuit with a wrong reading written by hand: the pin calls `ds_over_s` on the same deck with `reduce = ["makeposseq", "edit <element> <property>=<wrong value>", ...]`, so the twin differs from the reduction only in the value the pin names (the mean over four conductors, the other form, a star at zero volts, today's value). The pin asserts the twin's `dS/S` above the stated floor, so the pin fails when the code takes the wrong reading.
- Every pin runs in both lanes. Every refusal pin asserts the full message, its code `None`, that `errors()` holds exactly that one new entry, and that the circuit is unchanged: `balance::saved_circuit(dss, tag) -> String` (added by MPS-01: `save circuit dir=<std::env::temp_dir()/dss_posseq_saved/<tag>>` into the same directory, cleared before each save, then the text of every file written, sorted by file name, with the file names) is byte-identical before and after, `positive_sequence` is false, and `balance::solve_after_refusal(dss, when) -> Complex64` (added by MPS-01: it solves, asserts that the solve adds no error to the entries already there, and returns the power of `vsource.source`) gives the source power of the solve before the command to T3. `balance::solve_clean` asserts an empty error list and is not used after a refusal.
- A step that moves an oracle-compared cell measures it first, adds the field-level ledger entry with its cause, and names the pin that holds the engine's value. No golden byte moves unless the step lists it. A step that adds or edits a ledger entry or a corpus case regenerates `tests/corpus/manifests/population.lock.json` with `python tmp/retro_fix/bin/gatelock.py --label "<tag> lock" -- cargo test -p dss-core --test population_lock` and `DSS_UPDATE_POPULATION_LOCK=1` in the environment, and commits it with the change.
- Registers that move with the ledger, in `crates/dss-core/tests/corpus_gate/ledger.rs`: a new entry with an `element` scope that selects `powers` or `losses` (or names no `channels`) adds its (case, channel) pair to `AGGREGATE_VALUE_ARMS_INHERITING_THE_ELEMENT_LEDGER` (a fixed-length array whose length moves with it), and a new deck-wide `voltages` exclusion adds its triple to `BUS_ARRAY_SUPPRESSIONS`, each with a sentence naming the entry. A step whose Divergences add ledger entries lists that file.
- Population rails that move with a new corpus case: the deck count `decks.len() == 526` of `crates/dss-core/tests/corpus_gate/scratch.rs`, the forced populations of `crates/dss-core/tests/corpus_gate/scheduler.rs` (`FORCED_PROPS_POPULATION`, `FORCED_ELEMENT_EXTRAS_POPULATION`, `FORCED_PDELEMENTS_POPULATION`, `FORCED_BUS_POPULATION`, `FORCED_ZSC_POPULATION`, `FORCED_TOPOLOGY_POPULATION`, `FORCED_INC_MATRIX_POPULATION`, `FORCED_RUN_FILES_POPULATION`, `FORCED_DERIVED_POPULATION`, `FORCED_DI_POPULATION`), `population.lock.json`, `crates/dss-core/tests/props_r4133_evidence_lock.rs`, and the case counts `TESTING.md` states. A step that adds a case re-measures each tuple on its full gate (a live, non-large `capi_v0145` case moves the total and the `capi_v0145` member of every forcing rule that takes it by one), restates each with a sentence naming the new case, and updates the `TESTING.md` counts.
- The de-Pascalize rails of `crates/dss-core/tests/depascalize_metrics_gate.rs` stand at their ceilings: `CEILING_ONE_BASED_LOOPS = 105` (`for … in 1..=` under `src/elements/`, test code included) and `CEILING_FLAT_OFFSET = 15` (`* nconds`, `* ncond` in `dss-core/src`), and `p8_metric_no_raw_term_ref_indexing` (`term_ref[`) and `p1_metric_no_i32_constant_families_in_elements` (`pub const …: i32` in `elements/`) ban their forms outright. New code walks terminals and conductors through views and 0-based ranges and never multiplies by `nconds` (the flat leg index `nconds + k` of a terminal-2 conductor is an addition, which no rail counts).
- `PosSeqReduction` lives in `exec/make_pos_seq/analyse.rs` (MPS-01 creates it with `order`, MPS-02 adds `map` and `targets: Vec<Option<PosSeqTarget>>`, one slot per element). The stage files fill the slots and no later step edits `analyse.rs`: a stage that needs a field adds it to its own target struct in `elements/pos_seq/target/`.
- A step-3 pin whose rule changes is rewritten in place under a name that states the new rule (section 0). "Fails at the step base" means the pin goes red when run on the tree of the step's `base`. The words "today" and "at the step base" in this plan's pin cells describe the base for the executor and never go into a test message or a doc comment, which state the rule as it stands.
- Test files of `exec/tests/make_pos_seq/` and their owners: `mod.rs` (shared helpers, MPS-01), `refusals.rs` (MPS-01), `projection.rs` (created by MPS-01 with the Line test it moves there, then MPS-03), `pc.rs` (created by MPS-01 with the transformer test it moves there, then MPS-08), `map.rs` (MPS-02), `limits.rs` (created by MPS-03, then MPS-04 and MPS-05), `coupled.rs` (MPS-04), `chains.rs` (MPS-05), `sources.rs` (MPS-06), `controls.rs` (MPS-10), `feeder.rs` (MPS-09). Two lanes of one wave never edit the same file.

### MPS-01 — Analyse the whole circuit before MakePosSequence edits it, and stop with nothing changed on a refusal

<!-- MPS-STEP {"name": "mps01-analyse-then-apply", "description": "MakePosSequence analyses the whole circuit first, refuses with nothing changed, and does nothing on a positive-sequence circuit", "lane": "lane-mps01", "tag": "mps01", "state": "mps01", "prior": "makeposseq_matrix_fix", "base": "update", "task": "this section"} -->

**Tier, gate, after.** Executor opus/xhigh, the stages of section 3, full gate. After: nothing. Wave 1, alone.

**Files.** `exec/make_pos_seq.rs` becomes the directory module `exec/make_pos_seq/` (`mod.rs`, `apply.rs`, `analyse.rs`, `refuse.rs`). `elements/pos_seq.rs` becomes `elements/pos_seq/` (`mod.rs`, `balance.rs`, `project.rs`, `legs.rs`). `exec/tests/make_pos_seq.rs` becomes `exec/tests/make_pos_seq/` (`mod.rs`, `refusals.rs`, `projection.rs`, `pc.rs`). `elements/traits.rs` (`pos_seq_refusal`, `pos_seq_legs`). `elements/pd/mod.rs` (the constant `ABORTING_SOLUTION` beside `matrix_order_refusal`). The file of each class's `impl CktElement` block, where its `make_pos_sequence` lives, for one-line `pos_seq_legs` (and `pos_seq_refusal`) methods: `elements/pd/line/solve.rs` (`pos_seq_refusal` only), `elements/pd/reactor/solve.rs`, `elements/pd/capacitor/solve.rs`, `elements/pd/fault.rs`, `elements/pd/gic_transformer/solve.rs`, `elements/pc/load/accessors.rs`, `elements/pc/generator/accessors.rs`, `elements/pc/pvsystem/accessors.rs`, `elements/pc/storage/accessors.rs`, `elements/pc/windgen/accessors.rs`, `elements/pc/ind_mach012/accessors.rs`, `elements/pc/vccs/accessors.rs`, `elements/pc/vs_converter/accessors.rs`, `elements/pc/upfc/accessors.rs`, `elements/pc/gic_line/solve.rs`, `elements/pc/gic_source/solve.rs`, `elements/pc/vsource/solve.rs`, `elements/pc/isource/solve.rs`. `elements/pd/fault/tests.rs`. The path mentions of `exec/make_pos_seq.rs`: `exec/command.rs`, `elements/pc/storage/tests.rs`, `crates/dss-core/tests/props_r4133_pins.rs`, `tests/corpus/props_r4133/README.md`. `exec/tests/topology.rs`. `crates/dss-core/tests/props_r4133_replay.rs`, `crates/dss-core/tests/props_r4133_evidence_lock.rs`. `tests/corpus/modes/makeposseq/makeposseq_pc.dss`, `tests/corpus/modes/makeposseq/makeposseq_pc_repeat.dss` (new), `tests/corpus/modes/manifest.json`, `tests/corpus/ledger.json`, `tests/corpus/manifests/population.lock.json`, `crates/dss-core/tests/corpus_gate/scratch.rs`, `crates/dss-core/tests/corpus_gate/scheduler.rs`, `crates/dss-core/tests/corpus_gate/ledger.rs`, `TESTING.md` (the case counts), `docs/upgrade/DIVERGENCES.md`, `docs/phase-records/retro-fixes.md`, `STATUS.md`, `investigations/to_opendss/`.

**Scope by item.**
1. Splits first, byte-faithful by `SPLITTING_RULES.md` and committed alone: the applier (`apply_pos_seq_plan`, `apply_pos_seq_actions`, `build_pos_seq_ctx`, `resolve_pos_seq_info`) into `exec/make_pos_seq/apply.rs`, the test helpers `elements::pos_seq::balance` into `elements/pos_seq/balance.rs`, the command tests into `exec/tests/make_pos_seq/mod.rs`, with `makeposseq_converts_transformer_and_disables_off_phase1` moved to `exec/tests/make_pos_seq/pc.rs` and `makeposseq_strips_line_node_extension_and_is_idempotent` to `exec/tests/make_pos_seq/projection.rs`, so the two lanes of wave 3 edit different files. Every mention of the path `exec/make_pos_seq.rs` (in `exec/command.rs`, `elements/pc/storage/tests.rs`, `tests/props_r4133_pins.rs`, `tests/corpus/props_r4133/README.md`) is rewritten to the item it means (`exec::make_pos_seq::apply::apply_pos_seq_actions`), without a line number.
2. The pipeline in `exec/make_pos_seq/mod.rs::Dss::do_make_pos_seq`:
   1. No circuit: return.
   2. `ckt.positive_sequence` is true: return, nothing changed, no message (decision D8).
   3. `self.analyse_pos_seq()` (in `analyse.rs`, beside `struct PosSeqReduction`, which holds `order` in this step) returns `Result<PosSeqReduction, PosSeqRefusal>`. It reads the circuit and edits nothing (the aux parser is the only state it may touch).
   4. `Err(refusal)`: push `DssDiagnostic::msg(refusal.message(), None)` through the command error path (`self.errors.push`) and return. Nothing was changed.
   5. `Ok(reduction)`: set `ckt.positive_sequence = true`, then apply every element of `reduction.order` through the existing applier: every PD and PC element in creation order, then every control and meter in creation order (decision D16), enabled and disabled alike (decision D9). An `Err` of the edit path during the apply follows section 1.12 (stop, the message, a `debug_assert!`).
   In this step the analysis runs the element checks of item 3 over every circuit element in creation order and builds `reduction.order`. Later steps add the map, the targets and the writes to `PosSeqReduction`, in the stage order of section 1.12.
3. `exec/make_pos_seq/refuse.rs`: `enum PosSeqRefusal` with one variant per case and pattern of section 1.12 (all of them, so later steps only construct them) and `fn message(&self) -> String` producing the texts of section 1.12 exactly. This step constructs three:
   - Case 6 and every other reason the element's own solve refuses it: a new trait method `elements::traits::CktElement::pos_seq_refusal(&self) -> Option<String>` (default `None`) returns the element's solve message. Every class whose solve can refuse implements it: `Reactor`, `Capacitor` and `Fault` by calling their inherent `refusal()`, the `Line` by the two texts its `calc_yprim` pushes, the singular series impedance (`Matrix Inversion Error for Line "<name>". Invalid impedance specified. Aborting solution.`, code 183) found by inverting its total `Z` at the base frequency, and the impedance a geometry or spacing cannot build (the message of `make_z_from_geometry` or `make_z_from_spacing`), found by running that build at the base frequency on a clone of what it mutates, so `&self` stays unchanged. A class that gains a solve refusal later (queue step 10 may add some) implements it in the same way. The command message is `MakePosSequence: ` followed by that message with its trailing `ABORTING_SOLUTION` (`" Aborting solution."`, the constant in `elements/pd/mod.rs` that `matrix_order_refusal` now formats its text with) removed by `strip_suffix`, then ` Nothing was changed.`. An element the solve refuses has no stamp, so there is nothing to project.
   - Case 8: a Fault of one or two phases.
   - Case 7: a Fault whose stamped conductance (its `gmatrix`, or `1/r` on each phase) fails `elements::pos_seq::project::is_passive` (section 1.12, cyclic Jacobi on `(G + Gᵀ)/2`, smallest eigenvalue `>= -1e-12 · max |G_ij|`). The message names `gmatrix` or `r` as the element was given.
4. A new action `PosSeqAction::SetText(usize, String)` in `elements/pos_seq/mod.rs`, replayed by the applier through `ClassProps::edit_property` (the property edit path of a DSS `edit`, side effects included), wrapped in a single edit like the other `Set*` actions. Later steps write matrices and buses with it. This step adds it with a unit test and uses it nowhere else.
5. `balance::stiff_at`, `balance::solve_clean_at`, `balance::ds_over_s`, `balance::saved_circuit` and `balance::solve_after_refusal` with the signatures and contracts of the conventions of section 4. In `elements/pos_seq/project.rs`: `lu_solve(a: &CMatrix, b: &CMatrix) -> Option<CMatrix>` (dense complex LU with partial pivoting, one column of `b` per right-hand side, `None` on a zero pivot) and `inverse(a: &CMatrix) -> Option<CMatrix>` (`lu_solve` against the identity), the same arithmetic in both lanes, with a unit pin. Every later solve and inversion of this plan uses them (section 3).
6. Legs (section 1.2 "Data"): `PosSeqLegKind { Winding, Through, Lumped }` and `PosSeqLeg { kind, winding: usize, phase: usize, plus: usize, minus: Option<usize>, v_leg: f64, s_leg: Complex64 }` in `elements/pos_seq/mod.rs` (`minus: None` is a leg to earth), the trait method `CktElement::pos_seq_legs(&self) -> Option<Vec<PosSeqLeg>>` (default `None`), and in `elements/pos_seq/legs.rs` the builders the classes call, conductor indices running over all terminals in order (terminal 2's conductor `k` is `nconds + k`):
   - wye on one terminal: leg `k = (k, Some(N))` for `k < N`, the neutral conductor `N` (a one-phase wye element: `(0, Some(1))`),
   - delta on one terminal: `N >= 3` leg `k = (k, Some((k + 1) mod N))`, `N = 1, 2` leg `k = (k, Some(k + 1))` with the extra conductor,
   - a wye element with no neutral conductor that stamps a floating star (IndMach012): its line-to-line pairs as delta,
   - per conductor to earth, for an element with `nconds = nphases` that injects or draws at each conductor against earth (VCCS, the AC conductors of a VSConverter): leg `k = (k, None)`,
   - branch between two terminals: leg `k = (k, Some(nconds + k))`.
   Classes: Load, Generator, PVSystem, Storage, WindGen, IndMach012 by their connection (`Lumped`), VCCS and the AC conductors of a VSConverter to earth (`Lumped`), Reactor and Capacitor wye as branches and delta as delta legs (`Lumped`), Fault and GICTransformer as branches (`Lumped`, the GICTransformer winding by winding as its stamp walks them), GICLine, UPFC and GICsource as branches (`Through`), and a Vsource or Isource as branches (`Through`) only when its terminal 2 lies on nodes other than earth (section 1.2 "Data"): the circuit's own Vsource and a default Isource have no legs. `v_leg`: the rated leg voltage (`kV/√3` for a wye element of two or three phases rated line to line, `kV` for a delta leg and for one phase), 0 where the class has no rated voltage. `s_leg`: the complex power the leg draws at `v_leg` in the admittance the element's own Y stamp uses (its nominal admittance, generation negative, a `kvar` Capacitor `-j kvar/N`, a `kvar` Reactor `+j kvar/N`), 0 for a branch given by impedance or matrix. Transformer and AutoTrans keep the default until MPS-02.
   The same files get the trait method `CktElement::pos_seq_pattern(&self) -> Option<Vec<Complex64>>` (default `None`): the unit phasors, in conductor order, that the model of an element whose model depends on the order puts on its phase conductors in a balanced positive-sequence state (section 1.4 item 3). Vsource `(1, a², a)`, `(1, -1)` or `(1)` by its phase count, Isource `(1, a², a)`, `(1, a²)` or `(1)`, VSConverter `exp(-j 2π k / n_ac)` over its AC conductors, IndMach012 `(1, a², a)`, and a three-phase PVSystem or Storage with `Balanced=yes` or Generator of `model=7` with `Balanced=yes` `(1, a², a)`. Every other class returns `None`. MPS-02 reads it.
7. Corpus: `tests/corpus/modes/makeposseq/makeposseq_pc.dss` loses its second `makeposseq` and `solve`, so it converts once and its cells stay oracle-compared as today, and the new case `makeposseq_pc_repeat.dss` (the same circuit with the second `makeposseq` and `solve`, `engines` as the pc deck) holds the repeat. Its manifest entry, its lock line, its exclusions and the population rails of the section 4 conventions (the deck count of `corpus_gate/scratch.rs` to 527, the forced populations of `corpus_gate/scheduler.rs` re-measured and restated, the `TESTING.md` counts) are this step's. `exec::tests::topology::a_second_makeposseq_keeps_the_circuit_connected` compiles the repeat deck and its doc states the mechanism it pins: the guard of item 2 leaves the second conversion without effect, so the walk answers the same.
8. Decision D16 reachability: with epri-worker on r4133, the step tries to define a CapControl, an EnergyMeter and a Monitor before the element they name, and to create an element again after its control. It records the outcome (section 2, D16) and writes report 101 only if a deck reaches the old order.
9. DIVERGENCES: a new entry for this step beside the step-3 row, and in the step-3 row the sentence of its Exclusion paragraph about the `rp` reactor is rewritten to say that no golden or ledger entry reads the loss of the unequal or short steps or of the `rp` reactor (decision D19, `tests/golden/props/reactor.json` reads one back without a solve).
